# Elapsed — Product Roadmap

> **The single, living roadmap for Elapsed.** It says what we build next, what is in progress, and what is done. It is updated in place as the product evolves. Never start a second roadmap file.
>
> **Revision:** 18 · **Last updated:** 2026-10-12 · **Capacity:** 10–15 h/week
> **Stage:** a live multi-tenant product with **10 customers**. The active forward plan is the [Provider-Neutral Roadmap (N1–N8, plus the parallel tracks N9 and N10)](#active-roadmap--provider-neutral-platform-n1n8). The [historical Phases 0–7](#historical-phases-07) are kept below as the record of completed work.
> **Design records:** the per-phase implementation plans (`01`–`10`) and the two N9 legal-review documents were consolidated into this file in Rev 14 and deleted. What is still needed to continue the work is in [Design Records](#design-records-migrated-from-the-removed-implementation-plans-rev-14) below, which also says how to read the old "plan NN §x" citations. **This roadmap owns order, status, decisions and design records.** Never start a second planning file; the only other planning document is [`d29-zoho-desk-due-diligence.md`](d29-zoho-desk-due-diligence.md) (see [Documentation lifecycle](#documentation-lifecycle)).
> **Start here, then read only what the task needs.** The [document map](#documentation-lifecycle) says which other document to open for which task. Do not read every Markdown file or every historical plan by default.
> **History:** work delivered before the N-series (build steps 0–44), the pre-build strategy documents and the Rev 4 boards were removed in Rev 12; the product positioning and guardrails they held are in [Product Positioning & Guardrails](#product-positioning--guardrails), and the files remain in git history (last present at commit `4829341`). Parked ideas are listed under [Out of scope](#out-of-scope-for-now).

## How This Roadmap Works

### Workflow

1. **Define.** Upcoming product work is written here as a task inside a phase, or in [Next Product Work](#next-product-work) if it isn't scheduled yet. From Rev 5, active phases are the N-series; the how of each is in its [design record](#design-records-migrated-from-the-removed-implementation-plans-rev-14) (Rev 14: the separate plan files were removed). Tasks keep their status and evidence here.
2. **Implement.** Take the next unchecked task in the current phase, in order unless the task says otherwise. Mark it 🔄 while it's being worked on.
3. **Complete.** Tick the checkbox only when the task is **implemented and verified**: its "Done when" is met, tests pass, and CI is green. Add the date, e.g. `(done 2026-09-24)`.
4. **Close the phase.** When every task in a phase is ticked, change the phase status to ✅ with the date, in both the phase heading and the [Phase overview](#phase-overview), and update the [Status Board](#status-board).
5. **Continue.** When the current roadmap is finished:
   - Move finished phases into [Completed Phases](#completed-phases).
   - Promote items from [Next Product Work](#next-product-work) into new numbered phases.
   - Bump the revision and add a line to the [Changelog](#changelog).
   - Don't create a new file.

### Git Branch Strategy

Each Phase has its own dedicated Git branch.

#### Branch naming

Use:

`phase/<phase-number>-<short-name>`

Examples:

- `phase/0-safe-foundation`
- `phase/1-correct-sla-commitments`
- `phase/2-reliable-zendesk-jira-connections`

From Rev 5, active-roadmap phases use `phase/n<number>-<short-name>` (e.g. `phase/n1-provider-neutral-core`, `phase/n8-<item>` for a triggered scale item), and [Production Hygiene](#production-hygiene) tasks use `hygiene/<id>-<short-name>` (e.g. `hygiene/h-4-engine-spot-check`). Branches are created only when the work starts.

#### Branch rules

- All tasks belonging to a Phase must be implemented on that Phase's branch.
- Never implement Phase work directly on `main`.
- Create the Phase branch from the current `main` only after the previous Phase has been completed and merged.
- Do not start the next Phase branch while the current Phase is still open. (Exception for N9, below.)
- Keep the Phase branch focused on that Phase's tasks. Do not mix unrelated work into it.
- Push the Phase branch to the remote as work progresses. Individual tasks do not require separate PRs, **but smaller, reviewable PRs are permitted whenever they improve reviewability** (Rev 8): stack them into the phase branch, or open short-lived task branches that merge into it. N9 is planned this way.
- When all tasks in the Phase are implemented and verified, open a PR from the Phase branch into `main`.
- The Phase PR must pass remote CI and all required checks before it can be merged. **Rev 8 accuracy note:** the CI workflow ([`.github/workflows/ci.yml`](../.github/workflows/ci.yml), commit `539f9fa`) currently runs only for pushes to `testing` and pull requests that target `testing`. A pull request into `main` runs no remote CI, so this rule cannot be met as literally written. Until the owner decides a different pre-merge verification policy (an open, separate decision; CI triggers are deliberately not changed here), a PR into `main` is verified locally with `pnpm type-check`, the relevant build and focused checks, and the full suite runs when the owner asks for testing or when `main` is merged into `testing`, as [`CLAUDE.md`](../CLAUDE.md) says.
- **Testing branch (Rev 8 clarification).** `testing` stays based on `main`. `main` and `testing` are never merged into each other automatically. Test work is done on `testing` when the owner explicitly asks, and no mirrored test PR is created for each implementation PR.
- Merge the completed Phase PR into `main` before creating the next Phase branch.
- After the merge, update the roadmap with the Phase completion date and final merge commit.
- Never delete or recreate the roadmap to reflect Git progress; update this file in place.
- **Documented exceptions (Rev 6, 2026-10-05).** The rules above were not followed for **N3** (freshness, commit `eee09a7`, 2026-10-02) and **N6.1–N6.4** (entitlements and trial lifecycle, commit `94254cb`, 2026-10-02): both landed directly on `main` with no phase branch of their own (N6.1–N6.4 arrived inside the N5 merge). N6's billing steps N6.7–N6.10 followed the rules on `feature/billing` (PR #39). The final merge commit is recorded on a phase only where the roadmap names one; the phase PR numbers (N1 #33, N2 #35, N4 #36, N5 #37, billing #39) are the record for the others.
- **Documented exception (Rev 8, 2026-10-09): N9.** `phase/n9-custom-ticket-provider` is started while N1–N6 still have open items (every one blocked on deployment, a host run, owner data or a third-party account, not on code) because N9 is a parallel track that needs only N2, N3 and D31. The owner approved the N9 branch and the smaller-PR convention on 2026-10-09. Creating the branch authorises documentation only; implementation needs the owner's separate approval of the Rev 8 documentation.
- **Documented exception (Rev 9, 2026-10-09): N10.** `phase/n10-integration-control-center` is started while N9 is still open, for the same reason: N10 is a parallel track (needs N4, N9.8 and D33). The owner approved the branch, the PR to `main`, and syncing the implementation to `testing` only as far as the focused N10 tests need (D33 ruling 8).

#### Phase lifecycle

```text
main
  ↓
phase/0-safe-foundation
  ↓
implement Phase 0 tasks
  ↓
commit + push as work progresses
  ↓
all Phase 0 tasks complete and verified
  ↓
open PR → main
  ↓
remote CI + required checks
  ↓
merge PR → main
  ↓
Phase 0 = ✅
  ↓
phase/1-correct-sla-commitments
  ↓
implement Phase 1 tasks
  ↓
commit + push as work progresses
  ↓
all Phase 1 tasks complete and verified
  ↓
open PR → main
  ↓
remote CI + required checks
  ↓
merge PR → main
  ↓
Phase 1 = ✅
  ↓
phase/2-reliable-zendesk-jira-connections
  ↓
implement Phase 2 tasks
  ↓
commit + push as work progresses
  ↓
all Phase 2 tasks complete and verified
  ↓
open PR → main
  ↓
remote CI + required checks
  ↓
merge PR → main
  ↓
Phase 2 = ✅
```

#### Task completion verification

A task must not be marked `[x]` based on local implementation alone.

Before marking a task as done, verify all of the following:

- The task's `Done when` criteria are satisfied.
- The implementation is committed on the correct Phase branch.
- Required local tests and type-check pass.
- If the task or Phase requires remote verification, the Phase branch has been pushed to the remote.
- Remote CI is green when CI verification is required.
- If any required verification is still pending, keep the task unchecked and record the current state in its Status line.

Only mark `[x]` when the task is fully implemented and all required verification is complete.

### Legend

| Mark          | Meaning                                                                                                                                      |
| ------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `- [ ]`       | Not started                                                                                                                                  |
| `- [ ] 🔄`    | In progress (only one or two at a time)                                                                                                      |
| `- [ ] ⛔ D#` | Blocked by an open [decision](#product-decisions)                                                                                            |
| `- [x]`       | Done: implemented **and** verified                                                                                                           |
| `- [~]`       | Built, merged to `main` and verified locally, but a closing condition written on the task is still open (deployment, a replay, a live check) |
| `- [x] ~~…~~` | Dropped; the reason is written inline                                                                                                        |
| ⬜ / 🔄 / ✅  | Phase status: not started / in progress / completed                                                                                          |
| 📦            | Phase closed as historical (Rev 5): open tasks moved to [Production Hygiene](#production-hygiene), left unchecked in place                   |
| ⏸             | Phase starts on a trigger or recorded demand, not on a date                                                                                  |

**Task tags:** `P0` blocking · `P1` MVP · `P2` post-launch · `P3` nice to have. Kind is one of: Feature, UX, Bug, Security, Reliability, Testing, Infra, Docs. Layer: **A** exists but broken · **B** exists but incomplete · **C** not built yet.
**Refs** such as `E-4` or `I-1` point to [Appendix A — Audit Findings](#appendix-a--audit-findings).

### Rules

- **Fix before build.** Don't start a phase while an earlier phase still has an open `P0`.
- **Phase order is a dependency chain.** Phase 0 → 1 → 2 → 3, then 4 (needs Phase 1 semantics final) → 5 → 6 (needs the Phase 1 import data and the Phase 4 editors) → 7. Tasks inside a phase can be reordered when they don't depend on each other.
- **Active roadmap order (Rev 5):** N1 → N2 → N3 → N4 → N5 → N6 → N7 → N8. N6 and N8 start on their triggers; N7 (Zoho Desk, D29) on an explicit go-ahead. Production Hygiene runs alongside, except H-1/H-4, which gated N1 and were closed on 2026-09-30.
- **Replay gate (D24).** No change to `@sla/core`, `@sla/commitments`, a normalizer, correlation or customer/case identity merges without a replay/diff run on a restored backup showing zero unintended status / `breachedAt` differences. Intentional differences are classified and approved in the phase's plan.
- **Engine semantics freeze after Phase 1.** A later phase that finds an engine problem goes back through a decision, then a test, then code. No quiet fixes inside UI work.
- **No real customer account is connected before task 2.1** (token encryption). _(Historical: satisfied; 10 customers are live.)_
- **Product constraints:**
  - No AI.
  - ~~Zendesk is the Case source of truth.~~ **Rev 5 (D19):** a case's own ticket-source integration is its source of truth.
  - Work trackers are the engineering leg only.
  - Provider-specific knowledge stays at the adapter boundary. No general-purpose connector SDK, no dynamic plugin framework, no provider-specific SLA engine (D16, amended by D31: the single declarative `custom` ticket-source engine is the only exception).
  - Raw events stay replayable, normalized events are derived, evaluations are immutable.
  - Store events, never computed time.
  - No blame language.
  - Customer ≠ Requester.
  - Conversation ≠ Activity Timeline.
  - Multi-tenant everywhere.
  - Deterministic behavior.
  - No unnecessary architecture.

### Documentation lifecycle

This roadmap is the single live product roadmap. Never start a second one, and never track the same task status in two files.

**Document map: what exists and when to read it.** Open a row's document only when the task touches that area.

| Document                                                                                                | Purpose                                                                                                        | Read when                                                       |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `implementation-plans/ROADMAP_Product.md` (this file)                                                   | Order, status, decisions, open work, guardrails                                                                | Always, first                                                   |
| `implementation-plans/ROADMAP_Product.md` → [Design Records](#design-records-migrated-from-the-removed-implementation-plans-rev-14) | Per-phase how for N1–N10 (replay gate, contract, freshness, authorization, entitlements, N7, N8, N9, N10) and the N9 legal-review gate status | Working on an open task of that phase; N9 or N10 work |
| `implementation-plans/d29-zoho-desk-due-diligence.md` | The due diligence behind D29 and the N7 technical kill criteria (§13) | N7 only |
| `docs/integration-availability.md` | Operator runbook for provider availability (D33, N10) | N10-F1 or provider availability |
| `docs/customer-guide.md`                                                                                | Customer-facing product guide                                                                                  | A user-visible behavior changes                                 |
| `docs/deployment.md`                                                                                    | Self-host configuration, migrations, backups, security notes, multiple workers, observability, common mistakes | Deploying or operating                                          |
| `docs/production-backup-runbook.md`, `docs/n2-replay-runbook.md`                                        | Host backup, and the production-backup replay (the D24 gate procedure)                                         | Before a migration or release; before an engine-adjacent change |
| `docs/data-retention-and-on-call.md`                                                                    | What is kept, deletion, on-call                                                                                | Retention, deletion or incidents                                |
| `docs/capacity-limits.md`, `plans/performance-plan.md`                                                  | Measured capacity limits; the performance ground rules that source comments cite                               | Performance or capacity work                                    |
| `docs/custom-beta-deployment.md`, `docs/custom-provider-deletion-recovery.md`                           | Custom REST Beta deployment record; deletion-guard recovery                                                    | Custom REST Beta operations                                     |
| `docs/h-phase-close-out.md`, `docs/h4-sla-spot-check.md`                                                | Owner actions and closing evidence for the H items                                                             | An H item, or the "Up next" owner steps                         |
| `docs/validation/server-validation-master.md`, `docs/validation/evidence/`                              | Owner validation ledger and benchmark evidence (records; do not delete)                                        | Validation, D-01 or ledger references                           |
| Package and app READMEs                                                                                 | Local usage of that package                                                                                    | Working in that package                                         |

**Rules for every task.**

1. Start with this roadmap to understand the current product state and priorities.
2. Read only the additional documents the task needs, and follow direct references from the roadmap.
3. Do not recursively read every Markdown file or every historical plan.
4. If a task reveals outdated documentation, update or remove it as part of the task.
5. Do not create a new document when an existing authoritative document can be updated. Task-specific plans are temporary.
6. When implementation conflicts with existing documentation, identify and report the conflict **before** changing the documented decision. After approval, update the documentation to match the new decision.
7. Never remove unresolved work or important decisions to reduce the file count.

**Closing a task or phase.** Migrate any enduring knowledge into this roadmap or the existing authoritative document, update this roadmap, and delete a task-specific plan **only if** nothing still depends on it: check references from this roadmap, other documents, source comments, scripts and CI workflows, and update or remove them in the same change. Point-in-time audits and spot-check reports follow the same rule once every finding is resolved or recorded.

**Never delete for being old:** legally, operationally or compliance-required records; evidence that closes an item with an accepted limitation; runbooks still in use; the validation ledger and its evidence files.

**Plan consolidation (Rev 14, 2026-10-11).** Plans `01`–`10` and the two N9 legal-review documents were migrated into [Design Records](#design-records-migrated-from-the-removed-implementation-plans-rev-14) and deleted at the owner's instruction; the roadmap is now the only planning file besides `d29-zoho-desk-due-diligence.md`. Old citations ("plan 09 §6.4", "plan 10 §7.3", "plan 01 §5", "plan 06 §7") in source comments, docs and the validation ledger map to the same-numbered section of the design records. The removed files are in git history (last present at commit `d0fdc78`). The legal-review hold is unchanged: see the [N9 legal-review gate](#n9-legal-review-gate-status-record-no-legal-review-was-performed-in-rev-14).

## Product Decisions

Decisions that change product behavior. Tick one when it is decided and write the outcome.

- [x] **D1** — Does a new version of the _same_ policy (override, Zendesk re-import, or an edit in the policy UI) change **active** commitments? **Decided: no.** Only a switch to a _different_ policy re-resolves. → 1.1, blocks 4.4
- [x] **D1b** — Does a calendar change on its own (a customer calendar reassigned, or a calendar edited) move active commitments? **Decided: no.** It applies to new commitments only. → 1.1, blocks 4.6
- [x] **D2** — Is a breach final? **Decided: yes.** A target increase never un-breaches a commitment. → 1.2
- [x] **D3** — After a reopen, does the time spent solved count toward Resolution? **Decided: no.** Matches Zendesk's own behavior — the solve-to-reopen interval is excluded, same treatment as a customer-caused pause. → 1.4
- [x] **D4** — Does closing a case end an open Next Reply cycle? **Decided: yes.** Closing cancels the open cycle; a reopen followed by a customer reply starts a new one. → 1.5
- [x] **D5** — First Response when a case is closed with no reply, and on agent-created tickets. **Decided:** a reply-less close is not "met" (it reports breached once past target, and finalizes at the close rather than evaluating forever); on an agent-created ticket the clock starts at the first customer message, not ticket creation. → 1.6
- [x] **D6** — Match imported Zendesk policies by Zendesk's own `position` order instead of specificity? **Decided: yes.** A positioned candidate always outranks an unpositioned one; ties (same position, or neither positioned) fall back to specificity. → 1.10
- [x] **D7** — Does on-hold pause Resolution? **Decided: no, for MVP.** On-hold (Zendesk's internal hold status) never pauses Resolution — pinned by a regression test and documented explicitly (not merely undocumented). → 1.7
- [x] **D8** — Deployment-level transactional email (`DEPLOYMENT_SMTP_*`) for invites, password resets and verification? **Decided: yes** (2026-09-22), after an audit of the existing SMTP implementation. `OrganizationEmailSettings` is per-org, opt-in, and never auto-provisioned at signup — so it has no config to send through for exactly the flows D8 covers: the first invite, a password reset for an owner who never opened Settings → Notifications, or verification at signup itself. Reusing it would make core account-recovery/access flows depend on an unrelated, optional config. Reuses `packages/email`'s existing `sendEmail`/`EmailConfig` transport unchanged — only a new config source, no new mail-sending code. → unblocks 5.1
  - **Update (2026-09-22):** a second audit — specifically of the original `OPS_ALERT_SMTP_*`, added independently for the stalled-worker alert — found its separation from a deployment-level transactional mailer was never a deliberate design choice; it predates `TRANSACTIONAL_SMTP_*` entirely and was only ever built to be independent of _customer-owned_ `OrganizationEmailSettings`. Both already call the same `sendEmail(config, message)`, so there was no technical reason for two deployment-owned SMTP transports. Consolidated into one shared config, renamed `DEPLOYMENT_SMTP_*` (no longer purely "transactional" once it also backs ops alerts) — see 5.1's updated status.
- [x] **D9** — Intercom in the MVP? **Decided: Beta.** Native policies (Phase 4) give Intercom orgs commitments.
- [x] **D10** — Show the assignee? **Decided: yes, display only.** Store the display name only, never used for analytics or scoring. _Confirm you accept storing agent names._ → 3.6
- [x] **D11** — Keep the validation-first gate from the pre-build validation plan (outreach recorded as 0 of 40) before scheduling launch? → scheduling only. **Closed 2026-09-29 (Rev 5): superseded.** The product has 10 live customers, so the validation-first gate and the first-customer [Launch Gate](#launch-gate) are historical. They are replaced by [Validation Metrics](#validation-metrics) and [Review Triggers](#review-triggers).
- [x] **D12** — How do native and Zendesk-imported policies coexist? **Decided: imported policies are read-only (target overrides only) and match first, by Zendesk position; native policies follow, by specificity** (the recommended option). → blocks 4.1

**Rev 5 decisions (2026-09-29, provider-agnostic re-baseline):**

- [x] **D13** — When a case's source integration is stale, send at-risk alerts with a clear stale-data marker (for example, “data stale since …”), but **hold breach alerts until the source is fresh again**. Once the source becomes fresh, re-evaluate the case and send a breach alert if the breach is confirmed. → **Unblocks N3.5**
- [x] **D14** — Adopt the **live pricing model** currently defined in `apps/web/src/modules/marketing/pricing/csr/PricingView.tsx`: **Starter $49, Team $149, Enterprise Custom**, using **seat-based pricing**. The Phase 18 pricing model ($79 / $149 / $249, escalation-based) is not adopted, and the obsolete `$299/$699` pilot pricing must not be reintroduced. → **Unblocks H-3 (amounts), N4.3 (plan names), and N6**
- [x] **D15** — Use **Zendesk + Jira** as the current provider pair for the 10 customers and replay/test data. Customer provider assignments are configuration/data, not architectural constraints, and may be changed later. If a customer is moved to another provider pair, re-run the affected replay and re-classify any resulting differences; adjust N5/N7 priorities if the new customer mix warrants it. → **Unblocks N1.2**
- [x] **D16** — Provider integration model. **Decided: ~~no generic connector framework~~ no general-purpose connector framework, no dynamic plugin loading, no provider-specific SLA engine.** One statically typed adapter contract with compile-time registries (N2), containing only what current code needs.
  - **Amended by D31 (2026-10-09).** The original wording was "no generic connector framework". It is narrowed, not removed: a general-purpose connector framework, SDK, plugin system or marketplace is still prohibited. The single exception is the declarative `custom` ticket-source engine of D31, which interprets validated configuration data and never executes customer code. "Containing only what current code needs" is waived only for that engine's authentication and pagination modes. Every other D16 constraint (static compile-time registries, no dynamic loading, no provider-specific SLA engine, provider knowledge only at the adapter boundary) is unchanged.
- [x] **D17** — Provider matrix roles. **Decided:** Zendesk + Jira = production baseline; Zendesk + Linear = matrix validation; Intercom + Jira and Intercom + Linear = provider-boundary proof. Intercom and Linear leave the Rev 4 "freeze" for the boundary work only. Passing tests never promotes a provider or pair; promotion out of Beta is a separate, explicit decision per pair.
  - **Amended 2026-10-05 (owner): Linear is promoted out of Beta.** The code and UI already reflect it (`beta: false` for Linear in `provider-presentation.tsx`; the Beta label was removed in commit `e487c64`, 2026-10-01). **Intercom and GitHub stay Beta.** The `{Zendesk, Linear}` and `{Intercom, Linear}` matrix pairs are unchanged: promoting a _provider_ does not promote a _pair_.
  - **Amended by D33 (2026-10-09): how a provider's stage is applied.** A provider's release stage (Stable, Beta, Coming Soon) is now persisted and changed by a platform operator in `/admin/integrations`, not in code. Promotion out of Beta is still a separate, explicit owner decision recorded here; the console only applies it. Seeded from today's state: Zendesk, Jira and **Linear Stable**; Intercom and GitHub Beta (open to all organizations); Custom REST Beta (allowlist).
- [x] **D18** — GitHub. **Decided: frozen, Beta, `code_host` role, never an SLA source.**
- [x] **D19** — Case source of truth. **Decided: the case's own ticket-source integration, not Zendesk globally.** Replaces Appendix D invariant 6 and the Rules line "Zendesk is the Case source of truth".
- [x] **D20** — Billing. **Decided: trigger-based (N6), not immediate.** Manual plan and status records first (N4).
- [x] **D21** — Scale. **Decided: trigger-based per item (N8), never speculative.**
- [x] **D22** — Freshness. **Decided: freshness is an explicit concern.** Last successful sync per integration, recorded on evaluations and shown to customers and operators (N3). Circuit breaking is built **only** if N3's measurements justify it.
- [x] **D23** — Customer identity. **Decided: normalized into `CustomerIdentity` (provider, kind, externalId)** instead of provider-specific `Customer` columns, and Case uniqueness includes the source integration (N1 expand, N2 contract).
- [x] **D24** — Replay/diff gate. **Decided:** existing customer behaviour must not silently change. Engine-adjacent changes need a zero-unintended-difference replay on a restored backup; intentional differences are classified, documented and owner-approved (procedure: [Replay/diff gate](#replaydiff-gate-d24)).
- [x] **D25** — Imported policies **do not count toward plan policy limits**. Plan policy limits apply only to policies created and managed natively in Elapsed; imported policies represent existing customer configuration and remain outside the limit. → **Unblocks N6.2**
- [x] **D26** — A tracker admin who is not an Elapsed member can connect the tracker through a **signed, single-use, organization- and provider-scoped connect link**. The link is short-lived and consumed after successful use; the tracker admin does not need an Elapsed membership. → **Unblocks N5.3**
- [x] **D27** — When a trial expires, the account enters a **restricted state**. Existing cases, SLA monitoring, alerts, dashboard/history access, and existing data remain active. Creation of new cases and new configuration is blocked until the customer upgrades. The customer receives an in-app banner, the owner receives an email, and the account is marked as trial-expired. → **Unblocks N6.4**
  - **Interpretation recorded 2026-10-05 (owner): "new cases" means newly ingested cases from connected providers.** Cases that already exist, and their monitoring, alerts and history, stay active.
  - ⚠️ **Implementation gap (documented, not changed):** the code does **not** enforce this. After a trial ends (and only while `WorkerSettings.entitlementsEnforced` is on, which is off by default) it blocks new members, integrations and native policies (HTTP 402) and sends the owner notice, but provider sync keeps creating and evaluating new cases. There is no ingestion gating anywhere in `apps/worker` or `packages/ingestion`. This also sits in tension with the N6 principle that monitoring is never stopped or degraded because of plan state. Closing the gap needs a separate, explicit task.
- [x] **D28** — **Billing and payment provider strategy:** Elapsed targets both **Egyptian and international customers**, so billing must not be coupled to a single payment provider.

  **Decision:** Use a **provider-agnostic billing/payment abstraction** that supports multiple payment providers and selects the appropriate provider based on market, currency, and supported payment method.

  **Egypt requirements:**
  - Credit/debit cards.
  - Vodafone Cash and other supported mobile wallets.
  - Fawry/reference-code payments where supported.
  - Local payment methods required for Egyptian customers.

  **International requirements:**
  - International credit/debit cards.
  - Recurring subscription payments.
  - International currencies where supported.
  - A provider suitable for international subscription billing.

  The subscription/plan domain must remain provider-independent. Payment providers are implementation details behind a common interface for checkout, subscription creation/updates, cancellation, payment status, and webhook handling.

  Example architecture:

  ```text
  Elapsed Billing Domain
          │
          ├── PaymentProvider interface
          │       ├── Egypt provider(s)
          │       │     ├── Cards
          │       │     ├── Wallets
          │       │     └── Fawry
          │       │
          │       └── International provider(s)
          │             ├── Cards
          │             └── Recurring billing
          │
          └── Subscription / Plan records
                └── Provider-independent
  ```

  **Provider selection (closed 2026-10-05):** **Paymob is selected as the initial sandbox/testing payment provider only.** This is **not** a production-provider commitment. The billing architecture stays provider-neutral so the provider can be replaced or supplemented later.

  **Production provider selection remains explicitly open.** It must be decided separately, before any production billing go-live, based on Egypt support, international availability, recurring billing, supported currencies, fees, settlement, webhook reliability, and business/legal requirements.

  The obsolete `$299/$699` pilot pricing remains excluded; pricing is governed by D14.

  → **D28 closed 2026-10-05: provider-agnostic billing architecture; Paymob is the sandbox/testing provider only; the production provider is still open. Closing D28 does not start N6.5: no Paymob code, SDK, credentials, adapter, webhook or UI exists, and none is added by this decision.**

- [x] **D29** — Adopt **Zoho Desk** as Elapsed's **ticket provider for the Egyptian market**. Zoho Desk is technically compatible with the existing provider contract and shared projector, with no required changes to the Elapsed core domain model. Integration will use Zoho's API for initial/backfill synchronization and incremental reconciliation, with webhooks used where the customer's Zoho edition supports them and polling/reconciliation retained as the correctness mechanism.

  **Implementation constraints:** Zoho API usage is subject to the customer's plan-based API credit quota, so large historical backfills must be bounded and/or performed in controlled time windows. Elapsed should not assume unlimited historical import. The initial integration should use a defined backfill window (for example, 90 days) and measure actual API consumption during the N7 sandbox spike.

  **Zoho-specific requirements:** OAuth-based connection, ticket/history/thread synchronization, status normalization, customer/requester mapping, webhook handling where available, polling/reconciliation for missed or unordered events, and protection against automation-generated replies being incorrectly interpreted as human first responses.

  Zoho Desk is the **Egypt-market ticket provider** for N7. The provider remains isolated behind the provider contract so other ticket providers can be added later without changing the Elapsed core model.

  → **Unblocks N7**

- [x] **D30** — Does Resolution pause on Pending? **Decided 2026-09-29: imported Zendesk policies never pause on Pending (they mirror Zendesk, whose `resolution_time` has no pause events and breaches exactly at start + target); native policies keep the `pending_customer` pause.** Found by H-4; implemented as H-12.

- [x] **D31** — Custom ticket provider (declarative engine). **Decided 2026-10-09 (documentation phase only; implementation awaits the owner's approval of that documentation).** _(Numbered D31 because D30 was already taken.)_ Add **one** statically registered provider, `custom` (role `ticket_source`), whose adapter interprets a versioned, **closed-schema** configuration. It amends D16 (see D16). Design record: [N9 design record](#n9-design-record). Phase: [N9](#phase-n9--custom-ticket-provider).

  **Constraints that stay (not negotiable under D31):** customer-supplied code, scripts, expressions and plugins are never executed; compile-time registries; the provider-neutral SLA engine (`@sla/core`) and the commitments and notifications packages stay provider-blind; provider knowledge stays at the adapter boundary; all access to the source system is read-only; raw events stay append-only and replayable; Customer ≠ Requester; Conversation ≠ Activity Timeline; tenant isolation everywhere. Any change to shared ingestion, commitment or notification behavior is subject to the D24 replay gate.

  **V1 scope:** one custom integration per organization (`Integration @@unique([organizationId, provider])` is unchanged), with configuration, versioning, adapter and UI designed so multiple instances can follow without redesign. JSON over HTTPS only. `GET`, plus `POST` only on endpoints explicitly designated as read-only search/query. Pagination: page, offset/limit, cursor, same-origin next URL, `Link` header. Incremental sync by updated-since with a configurable look-back. Authentication: API key in a header, Bearer, Basic, custom auth header. No OAuth. **API keys in query parameters are prohibited.** Required canonical fields: external ID, created at, current status. V1 custom fields: tags and channel only. No new canonical fields and no arbitrary attributes in native policy matching without a separate decision.

  **SLA modes:** _Full SLA_ (tickets plus reply data with reliable author roles; where the source has no reliable public/private field, an explicit owner acknowledgement that all returned comments are customer-visible is required and is never assumed) and _Resolution-only_. Status history is optional; transitions are **never** synthesized from polling observations, and `case_closed` is never fabricated. A terminal status without a reliable source-supplied closure timestamp **blocks Resolution tracking** for that configuration (`updatedAt` is never substituted). **Unsupported commitment kinds are excluded in the shared commitments pipeline, not hidden in the UI**, through a provider-blind `Integration.slaSupport` (null for every existing provider, so their behavior is unchanged; D24 replay and regression required). A current-status-only source carries visible notices (integration page and case detail) and an alert caveat for affected Resolution calculations in the first release. Full detail: N9 design record §5.

  **Safety limits (V1), all checked before projection so an aborted batch is never partly applied:** deletions above `max(3, 5% of live cases)`; failed records at least 3 and above 25% of the batch; at least 10 **and** more than 25% of existing live cases changing lifecycle state in one pass (Q11); a configurable live-case ceiling that is **provisional** and fails safely without truncating or partly rewriting (Q13: 20,000 is **not** approved, no ceiling is validated, and it is fixed only by the N9 design record §6.10 benchmark gate). Ingest: 120 s total wall-clock budget per run with every request bounded by the remaining time, 30 s per attempt, 50 pages and 5,000 tickets per run, 5 MB per response and 50 MB per run, 5 requests per second, JSON depth 20. Turning the Beta flag off stops new requests and retries, aborts an in-flight request within about 5 s, discards its response and preserves visible data. Deletion is authoritative only on a reliable source deletion signal or a verified re-fetch, never on absence from a list.

  **Third review (2026-10-09), Q11–Q16, recorded in N9 design record §2.1:** **Q11** the lifecycle guard aborts only when `R ≥ 10` and `R > 0.25 × L`, atomically and auditably. **Q12** a run that exhausts the 120 s budget is _partial, not successful_: progress is kept through the last fully completed page, `lastSuccessfulSyncAt` does not advance, status and reason appear in sync history, resumption never duplicates events or commitments, and D13(b) is unchanged (stale at-risk alerts keep the marker; breach alerts stay held). **Q13** 20,000 is not an approved ceiling; a benchmark gate (N9 design record §6.10) must pass first; until then the ceiling is provisional, configurable and fails safely; the incremental-poll / full-sweep design **remains a proposal** until correctness and replay behavior are demonstrated; raw-event pruning stays deferred (H-5). **Q14** existing commitments that become unsupported change only after a dry-run and explicit confirmation: never delete, cancel only unfinalized unsupported ones, preserve finalized history, idempotent, D24 replay checks. **Q15** no automatic override after a guard abort; an override needs an authorized actor (defined by R1 below), a recorded reason, a preview and a narrowly scoped, audited confirmation, and never bypasses tenant isolation, request limits, the wall-clock budget or security validation. **Q16** strict, authenticated per-value encryption with the existing `INTEGRATION_TOKEN_ENCRYPTION_KEY`, bound to organization, integration and field, only after compatibility and security verification; malformed ciphertext is rejected, never read as plaintext; secrets redacted everywhere; the key-rotation limitation is documented (owners re-enter custom credentials until a rotation mechanism exists); the encryption used by existing providers is not changed. The only owner decision still open is N9 design record §15.1 U2, which must be resolved before Beta activation; §15.2 lists proposed details that are not decisions.

  **Fourth review (2026-10-09), R1–R6, recorded in N9 design record §2.1:** **R1** the customer-side authority for an override is the organization owner or an explicitly authorized organization administrator; a platform operator never silently overrides a customer's guard, and an exceptional support-assisted override needs a separate permission path, explicit customer authorization, an audit record and a narrowly scoped confirmation (finding: only `owner` and `member` roles exist, so in V1 the owner is the only customer-side authority). **R2** an override never bypasses the failure-ratio guard, capacity ceiling, wall-clock budget, tenant isolation or security validation, and never forces a mass deletion the deletion guard rejected (that cleanup needs a separate reviewed workflow). **R3** a run ending only because its time or record budget was exhausted is `partial` and does not increment `consecutiveFailures`; partial runs and reasons are recorded separately; a real provider, authentication, transport or processing failure still follows the failure policy; partial runs never advance `lastSuccessfulSyncAt`; D13(b) is preserved; the behavior of a run with completed pages and record failures is documented (N9 design record §6.12). **R4** 5,000 is an explicitly unvalidated provisional guard limit from the existing capacity documentation, not a validated custom-provider capacity; Beta stays blocked until the benchmark fixes the ceiling. **R5** the real `runCommitmentPipeline` was inspected: it never recreates or restores a cancelled `first_response` or `resolution` commitment (a cancelled row counts as present), nothing cancels those two kinds today, and the Next Reply planner would silently restore a cancelled `next_reply` commitment with its old clock, so rollback never reactivates cancelled commitments and a restore needs a new reconciliation path (N9 design record §5.6). **R6** a per-run cap is `partial`; activation is blocked for a source with no usable incremental cursor that cannot finish its initial full pass within one run; customer-facing states keep an import distinguishable from a fault (N9 design record §6.13). The optional `partial` outcome on `IngestResult` and the `cycle.ts` branch are shared-code changes kept in the implementation plan with focused regression and D24 replay where applicable.

  **Data and retention:** a whitelist projection of mapped fields and required identifiers is stored, each stored payload capped at 64 KB; sample responses are never stored server-side; drafts and draft secrets expire after 14 days; sync-run history is kept 30 days. **Pruning of superseded raw snapshots is deferred and depends on H-5**: no raw event is deleted by this feature, and the latest snapshot a live case needs is always kept.

  **Rollout:** Beta (D17). An operator-controlled per-organization enable flag, default off, that never bypasses authorization or tenant isolation. A custom source counts against the existing support-integration allowance; plan limits and pricing do not change.
  - **Amended by D33 (2026-10-09): where the flag lives.** The per-organization flag is the Custom REST entry of the generic Beta allowlist (`IntegrationBetaAllowlist`); `Organization.customProviderEnabled` is migrated into it, kept unread for one release, then dropped (N10-F1). Its approved behavior (N9 design record §8.7) is unchanged. _(Rev 17, 2026-10-12: the owner removed N9.14-F1 and the backend block D33 ruling 3 had added for it; Custom REST availability now follows the same rules as every provider.)_

  **Deferred (each needs its own decision):** multiple custom integrations per organization, OAuth, webhooks, tracker or code-host roles, new canonical fields, arbitrary attributes in policy matching, templates or a marketplace, configuration import/export, policy or calendar import, a per-integration minimum polling interval (V1 follows the global worker tick), undelete of a soft-deleted case, and pruning of superseded raw snapshots (H-5).

  **Relationship to other decisions:** D29 (Zoho Desk, N7) remains a separate track and may be used as a validation target for the custom engine; it is not replaced. D30 matters here: custom sources use native policies, which keep the `pending_customer` pause, so a source without status history cannot reproduce that pause (N9 design record §5.3).

  → **Unblocks N9 (documentation phase written and its decisions recorded; the owner's final diff review is pending; N9.1 is blocked until the owner explicitly approves proceeding).**

- [x] **D32** — No-change syncs are not stored as history. **Decided 2026-10-09 (amends N9 design record §6.7, §6.12, §6.13; extends N9.9).** A successful sync that changed no data and recorded no failure creates **no** `IntegrationSyncRun`; failed, partial, aborted and ticket-failure runs are always stored with their codes, progress and details. "Last checked" is `Integration.lastSuccessfulSyncAt` and "last data update" is the new nullable `Integration.lastDataChangedAt` (additive migration, no backfill); neither is derived from history, and no-change runs never advance the latter. A data change is decided by before/after comparison in the shared projector (Case fields, Customer, events, deletions) plus link and import counts; the projector's writes and replay semantics are unchanged, so D24 holds (replay must show 0 differences). A persisted run is superseded by a later clean check (`lastSuccessfulSyncAt > run.finishedAt`), so an old failure cannot keep the page in **Needs attention**. Skipping the row does not remove the per-attempt `Integration` update or the projector's per-case upserts. Implemented as N9.15.

- [x] **D33** — Integration availability controls (Integration Control Center). **Decided 2026-10-09 (owner approved rulings 1–8).** Design record: [N10 design record](#n10-design-record). Phase: [N10](#phase-n10--integration-control-center). Pulls "feature flags" forward from the Later list of the N4 design record for integration providers only.

  **Model.** Each provider has an operator-controlled `enabled` switch, **independent** of its release stage, and a stage of **Stable, Beta or Coming Soon** ("Disabled" is not a stage). A Beta provider is open to all organizations or to an explicit per-organization allowlist. Percentage rollout and an "internal organizations" concept are out of scope. Persisted in `IntegrationAvailability` and `IntegrationBetaAllowlist`; provider registration stays static and compile-time (D16): a static catalog in `@sla/db` supplies names, categories and defaults, and only runtime availability is data.

  **Enforcement.** An integration operation runs only when its provider is **enabled and available to that organization**. One resolver answers that question for every path: connect, OAuth callback, connect links, OAuth-app configuration writes, manual backfill, webhooks, concierge exports, the Custom REST routes, worker ingest and the Custom REST in-flight check. Moving a provider to an allowlist or Coming Soon blocks operations for organizations that no longer qualify. Refusals carry one documented code (`integration_disabled`, `integration_coming_soon`, `integration_beta_restricted`; HTTP 403 for APIs, a redirect for OAuth GETs). Webhooks for an unavailable provider are acknowledged with **HTTP 200 and ignored**; the next poll after re-enablement recovers the changes from the stored cursor. Every check reads the database, so web and worker processes observe a change on their next check; no new infrastructure.

  **Preservation.** Disabling never deletes or rewrites integration rows, credentials, cursors, imported data, cases, events, commitments or customer records, and never changes `Integration.status`. Work on stored data (normalization, commitments, evaluation, alerts) continues and the source goes stale (D22, D13). Platform unavailability is shown as "Paused by Elapsed", distinct from a customer's disconnect; customers may still disconnect but not reconnect. Re-enabling resumes from stored state and never resumes a separate operator pause or a customer disconnect.

  **Authority and audit.** Only `PLATFORM_ADMIN_EMAILS` operators (no database-backed admin roles). Every change is one `AdminAuditLog` row with the operator, provider, before/after policy and a required reason; never a secret. Policy writes are compare-and-set on a version column (409 on a stale write).

  **Custom REST.** Amends D31's rollout line (see there): the per-organization flag becomes the Custom REST allowlist, with N9 design record §8.7's behavior unchanged. _(Rev 17, 2026-10-12: the backend enforcement of N9.14-F1 originally recorded here was removed together with the item, at the owner's instruction.)_ **Slack** is out of scope and unchanged. **Linear** is seeded Stable (D17 amendment).

  **Amendment D33-A1 (2026-10-10, owner), now moot:** it narrowed the N9.14-F1 block so that individual allowlist membership stayed possible. N9.14-F1 and its backend block were removed by the owner on 2026-10-12 (Rev 17), so there is nothing left to narrow; an allowlist add is scoped to that organization, validated and audited (`addToBetaAllowlist`) like any provider's. Production rollout configuration and production allowlists are unchanged. See the N10 design record §5.4.

  → **Unblocks N10.**

## Product Positioning & Guardrails

_Migrated in Rev 12 from the pre-build strategy documents (written 2026-09-05; removed because the product is built and live; last present in git at `4829341`). The validation-first plan, the `$299/$699` pilots and the kill criteria built around them are superseded (D11, D14) and are deliberately not carried over. Competitor facts were verified on 2026-09-05 and have not been re-checked._

- **Problem and wedge (W1, escalation SLA continuity).** When a ticket is escalated to engineering, the customer's clock keeps running while support's visibility stops, and the two systems produce different numbers for the same case. Elapsed keeps one honest clock across both and warns before the commitment breaks. Deterministic correlation through the official ticket-tracker link is what makes this feasible without fuzzy matching.
- **Ideal customer.** B2B SaaS, 80–400 employees, 6–25 support agents, 15–150 named accounts with contractual support targets (2–4 tiers × 3–4 priorities), a customer-facing helpdesk (Zendesk, or Intercom) plus a separate engineering tracker (Jira or Linear) and Slack. The qualifying metric is **40–400 escalations to engineering per month**; below about 40 the pain is anecdotal. Buyer: Head of Support or Director of Support Operations. Blocker: the VP Engineering, who approves the tracker grant, so every first-run view must show engineering something it wants (which escalations are ageing in its queue, which are misrouted).
- **Not the first vertical.** MSPs (their PSA already is the single source of truth, the segment is crowded, and recording one's own contractual breaches is a conflict of interest), financial services and healthcare (procurement and security review), agencies (price).
- **Positioning.** Lead with the escalation, not with "SLA": _"Your SLA clock doesn't stop when the ticket leaves Zendesk."_ Promise: one honest clock that survives the handoff, and sight of the leg support cannot currently see. Do not invent a category. Rejected framings: "Don't miss your SLA", "Prove who caused the breach", "the system of record for service commitments".
- **Neutral language, no exceptions** (this is the "no blame language" invariant):

  | Never                           | Always                                    |
  | ------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------- |
  | "Prove who caused the breach"   | "See where the time went"                 |
  | "Root cause: Engineering"       | "Longest leg: engineering queue — 2h 17m" |
  | "Delay attribution"             | "Time by stage"                           |
  | "Engineering breached its OLA"  | "Engineering leg exceeded its 2h target"  |
  | "Blame", "fault", "responsible" | "Contributing", "elapsed", "leg"          |

- **Evidence is a supporting feature.** It is used internally and weekly, to explain. Build it as a rendered timeline on the case page. No dispute packs, PDF exports, customer-facing portals or e-signature workflows until a paying customer asks twice, and never gate the timeline behind a higher tier.
- **Financial features stay out.** No service-credit, penalty or exposure calculation: credits are small and usually the sole remedy, contract interpretation is not computable, and a dashboard of "potential credits" is discoverable liability. The one allowed money-adjacent field is a manually entered account value or tier, used only to prioritise and sort. Revisit only after 10+ paying customers, a real contract corpus and an explicit customer request, as a paid professional-services conversation with a lawyer's review.
- **Onboarding principles.** No configuration before first value; ask for nothing the connected systems already hold; target under 15 minutes from sign-up to a findings screen, unattended; stream findings as the backfill computes; deliver partial value from the ticket source alone while tracker approval is pending; report link coverage honestly ("we could link 61% of your escalations") and never invent a link to improve the number.
- **Competitive landscape (verified 2026-09-05).** Closest alternatives: Deviniti's SLA+OLA app and SaaSJet on the Atlassian Marketplace, Celonis process mining for ITSM, ServiceNow's native OLA support, and helpdesk-native SLA reporting. The moat is positional (neutrality between ticket source and tracker), not technical: a helpdesk or Atlassian vendor could close it. Three of the six competitors named in the original concept did not verify (one could not be found, one is a general data-integration platform, one is status-page alerting).
- **Assumptions about the customer that are still unvalidated** (test them with customers; link coverage is measured by the product itself): that 40+ escalations per month is the right threshold; that the Head of Support controls the budget at this size; that these companies have written, contractual SLAs; and that the official ticket-tracker link is actually used rather than pasted URLs (the highest-leverage question: a negative answer changes the product).

# Current Stage (Rev 5, 2026-09-29)

> **Rev 6 update (2026-10-05).** The forward plan has been built to code-complete on `main`: N1 and N2 (provider-neutral core, contract and shared projector), N3 (freshness), N4 (Platform Admin), N5 (onboarding and retention) and N6 (entitlements and internal billing). What remains is deployment and verification, owner-only data and access, one held-out migration (N2.10) and the gated N6.5. See the Status Board for the exact list. The Rev 5 text below is kept as written.

**What changed.** The product is no longer at the "validate whether anyone wants this" stage. It runs **10 live customers** on a multi-tenant deployment. Three planning assumptions are therefore obsolete:

- **The validation-first plan** (pre-build strategy documents, removed in Rev 12): first five customers, CSV concierge, `$299/$699` paid pilots, and the kill criteria built around them. D11 is closed. It is replaced by [Validation Metrics](#validation-metrics) and [Review Triggers](#review-triggers).
- **The first-customer Launch Gate.** It is kept as history; its still-relevant items are now [Production Hygiene](#production-hygiene).
- **Zendesk as the architectural centre.** The Rev 4 target was "Production-ready MVP for Zendesk + Jira customers", with "Zendesk is the Case source of truth" as a rule. The new target is a provider-independent core (D16–D19).

**What the 2026-09-29 audit found.** The engine math (`@sla/core`: event folding, calendars, versioned policies, immutable evaluations) is provider-independent and pure. The system around it is Zendesk-centred:

- the core decides ticket-source vs. tracker by provider name;
- Jira and Linear correlate only to Zendesk ticket URLs, so an Intercom + Jira customer gets no engineering leg;
- `Customer` stores provider-specific identity columns;
- onboarding requires Zendesk;
- the worker and web branch per provider;
- there is no notion of data freshness.

The full violation list, with file references, was the V1–V14 table of the removed plan 01 (all fixed by N1.5–N1.15; the file is in git history at `d0fdc78`).

**How this roadmap is organised from Rev 5:**

1. **[Active Roadmap N1–N8, plus the parallel track N9](#active-roadmap--provider-neutral-platform-n1n8)**: the forward plan.
2. **[Production Hygiene](#production-hygiene)**: open items carried from Phases 6–7 and the Launch Gate, plus the facts N1 needs.
3. **[Historical Phases 0–7](#historical-phases-07)**: the completed record. Nothing there was deleted; task IDs such as `7.7` stay stable because code comments and commits reference them.

(Later: H-2 closed 2026-10-05; H-9 closed 2026-10-12, Rev 16.)

- Nothing else here blocks an N-phase.

    - **12 Resolution elapsed differences: H-12 / D30.** Stored evaluations of already-finished commitments still carry the old Pending pause. Elapsed's shortfall equals the time spent in `pending_customer`, ticket by ticket, to the second (tickets 17, 19, 20, 21, 45, 46, 47, 48, 49, 53, 59, 60: 16, 44, 8, 8, 216, 123, 72, 824, 5, 50, 3184, 45 s), confirmed by query.

The cause is deliberate in the code (`findFirstResponseEvent` is called without the D5b start bound for cycle derivation, see `packages/core/src/evaluate.ts`). **Owner decision 2026-09-30: recorded as a documented product semantic (D5b × Next Reply gating), not an engine defect.** Revisit if customers report duplicate reply alerts.

This run covers **one** tenant, the dev Zendesk sandbox, with 0 open commitments and a 24/7 calendar. **Not covered:** business hours, holidays, DST, open commitments and at-risk state, legs, and any live customer tenant. Risk accepted: an engine difference specific to those areas would first surface in N1's replay/diff gate or in production.

Not caused by N1, does not block it. Identify the cause (a rule fix since the row was written, e.g. H-11 / H-12, versus a data defect) and either repair or record why it stays. Counts only, no customer data. `P1 · Correctness · —`

Production therefore has nothing left for `backfill:breached-at` to update. **Not established:** why the original 9 rows (and the by-design skipped one) are gone. No backfill is recorded on production and none was run; removed fixture organizations are one possibility, but the evidence below places the 9 in the one non-fixture organization, so the two records conflict and are left unreconciled. The earlier "drift 0 / 0, so nothing is left" reading (ledger C-05b/C-13) was an inference from 58 commitments and is superseded by this query. **Counts, for the record:** 10 at the N1.2 baseline (2026-09-29 backup); 9 drifting commitments in later captures (see "Why 10 at N1.2 and 9" below, unverified); the backfill rehearsal on the restore (ledger C-10) considered 10 commitments with a NULL-`breachedAt` breached evaluation, i.e. 9 rows updated plus 1 skipped by design, and the skipped row is not part of the drift count. The evidence below is historical and is not re-verified by this closure.
  - **Cause found 2026-10-03; the repair is a host run, so this stays open.** _(Historical status; superseded by the closure note above.)_ None of the rows is an engine difference. The stored breached `Evaluation` has `breachedAt = NULL`, so any recomputed instant "disagrees" with it. `Evaluation.breachedAt` was added by migration `20260928105156_add_evaluation_breached_at` without a backfill in SQL. Its one-off script `pnpm --filter @sla/commitments backfill:breached-at` (`9bad1b5`) was never run on the host. Every breached evaluation with a NULL `breachedAt` was written on 2026-09-28 between 10:31 and 11:15, before the pipeline wrote the column; from 12:10 on, all 1,562 have it.
  - **Evidence (local, on a restore of `backups/pre-n3-elapsed_db-20261002T033846Z.dump` into `sla_restore_drill`, after `migrate deploy`; capture at `--as-of 2026-10-02T03:38:46Z`):** 9 drifting commitments, all Resolution, all in the one non-fixture organization, each with exactly 1 evaluation and a NULL `breachedAt`. For 6, the recomputed elapsed time equals the stored one. For 3, it is 216 s, 44 s and 5 s higher: the D30 Pending-pause change (H-12) on tickets already listed under H-4. For those 3, the backfill writes the current-rule instant, which is Zendesk's own breach time, while the row's `elapsedSeconds` keeps the old pause, as H-12 accepted for finished commitments. After `backfill:breached-at`, L1 compare against the pre-backfill capture shows **0 differences over 5,440 records, drift 0 status / 0 `breachedAt`** (was 0 / 9). A second run writes 0 rows.
  - **Why 10 at N1.2 and 9 since N2.11:** most likely one of the 7 First Response commitments that the H-11 repair deleted right after the N1.2 backup. Not verified: that backup is on the host only.
  - **One row stays, by design:** commitment `8000cdcb…` has a NULL-`breachedAt` breached evaluation (11:15:09, elapsed 1,201 s of 1,200), then `met` at 11:17:02. The close event (elapsed 1,179 s) arrived one poll later. That is a late-data correction, not a D2 violation, and it sent 0 notifications. The backfill skips it ("recomputed status is met"). The row is not the latest evaluation and the commitment is not `breached`, so neither the replay drift nor the dashboard (`getPersistedBreachedAt` reads only breached commitments) uses it.
  - **Customer effect until the run:** the dashboard's breaches-over-time chart falls back to `dueAt` for these 9 commitments (one organization).
  - **To close (host)** _(historical; no longer needed, see the closure note)_**:** `ENV_FILE=.env DB_NAME=elapsed_db ./scripts/backup.sh`, then run `pnpm --filter @sla/commitments backfill:breached-at` in the worker container against `elapsed_db`. Expect "Backfilled breachedAt for 9 Evaluation row(s)" and 1 skipped (`8000cdcb…`). Then a replay capture should report drift 0 / 0.
  - **Blocker (Rev 7): production host** _(historical; superseded 2026-10-10)_**.** The cause and the repair are proven on a restore (above); the 2026-10-05 replay on the production backup still shows the same 9 `breachedAt` drifts, as expected before the run.

# Active Roadmap — Provider-Neutral Platform (N1–N8)

**Intent:** audit and harden the existing provider boundary, move **only** the provider decisions that currently leak into the domain, and prove the boundary with replay/diff and the 2×2 provider matrix. **This is not a core rewrite.** Engine math, calendars, policy versioning, evaluation identity and every working behaviour stay as they are unless a replay shows an approved, intentional difference (D24).

**Provider matrix (D17).** Tests passing never promotes a provider or pair; promotion out of Beta is a separate decision.

```text
              Jira                         Linear
Zendesk       production baseline          matrix validation
Intercom      provider-boundary proof      provider-boundary proof
```

GitHub stays frozen and Beta (`code_host`, never an SLA source, D18). The third ticket source for N7 is Zoho Desk (D29). Other helpdesks are not candidates for a dedicated adapter unless a new decision says so; D31 (N9) is the configuration-only path for a helpdesk without an adapter, and it does not replace N7.

**Dependency chain:**

```text
Historical Phases 0–7 (production launch / existing work)
        ↓
Phase N1 — Provider-neutral core
        ↓
Phase N2 — Provider contract + shared projector
        ↓
Phase N3 — Provider isolation + freshness
        ↓
Phase N4 — Platform Admin + plan records
        ↓
Phase N5 — Customer onboarding + retention
        ↓
Phase N6 — Entitlements + billing        (starts on its trigger)
        ↓
Phase N7 — Third ticket source           (starts on recorded demand)
        ↓
Phase N8 — Scale                         (each item starts on its trigger)

Phase N9 — Custom ticket provider        (parallel track: needs N2, N3 and D31; not part of the chain above)
Phase N10 — Integration Control Center   (parallel track: needs N4, N9.8 and D33)
```

**Architectural constraints carried into every N-phase:**

- Store events, never computed time. Raw events stay replayable; normalized events are derived; evaluations are immutable.
- The SLA engine stays pure and deterministic.
- Provider-specific knowledge belongs at the adapter boundary.
- No general-purpose connector SDK, no dynamic plugin framework, no provider-specific SLA engine, no unnecessary abstraction (D16; D31 permits the one declarative `custom` ticket-source engine and nothing broader).
- Customer ≠ Requester. Conversation ≠ Activity Timeline.
- No blame-oriented language.
- Multi-tenant isolation everywhere.
