Callers assemble the two-entry static map: `apps/worker/src/case-ref.ts` (cycle, seed script) and `apps/web/src/lib/case-ref.ts` (Jira webhook route, Linear backfill route, `source-sync`). `apps/concierge` now imports `parseZendeskTicketId` from `@sla/zendesk`. The boundary allowlist is **empty**. Tests: recognizer unit tests for both adapters, new real-DB suite `tracker-link-resolution.test.ts` (resolver cases, Jira and Linear linking to an Intercom conversation), the Jira real-DB suites moved to a `correlateJira` helper; 8 real-DB files (63 tests) and the unit suites pass; type-check clean. **Live capture, Jira (2026-10-05):** Intercom's Jira integration writes nothing on the Jira issue (no remote link, issue link, label or description; `GET /issue/<key>/remotelink` returned `[]`), so the remote-link path above can never link an Intercom case to Jira. The only record is the `jira_issue_key` custom attribute on the Intercom object the issue was created from, usually a **Tracker ticket**. A Tracker is the shared internal ticket made from one or more customer conversations (Intercom's `linked_objects` names it on both sides; observed: one conversation with two trackers, and Intercom documents many conversations per tracker), so it is a link record like Zendesk's official Jira link, not a Case (decision D, approved 2026-10-05). `buildIntercomBatch` makes no Case for a tracker (existing ones are soft-deleted; raw events kept), and `correlateIntercomJiraKeys` (`packages/intercom/src/correlate.ts`, the Intercom adapter's `correlate`) links the key as a `certain`/`official_link` `CaseLink` onto every conversation whose `linked_objects` names that tracker, with the tracker id as evidence; a conversation or customer ticket carrying the key itself links its own Case. Only structural references count, never titles or timestamps. Clearing or changing the key, or unlisting the tracker, unlinks (`unlinkedAt`, nothing deleted), and the sweep also retires a link the first version wrote onto a tracker's own Case. The projector no longer reconciles `issue_linked` / `issue_unlinked` events away (they belong to the link projector), and Jira normalization now derives an issue's events onto **every** actively linked Case (it kept one Case per issue key and ignored `unlinkedAt`, which a shared tracker and a retired link both break). Test: `apps/web/test/intercom-jira-correlation.test.ts`. **Environment limits when the code was done (both since resolved; see "Closure (Rev 16)" below):** (1) the Intercom → **Linear** link and any remote-link URL an Intercom workspace may write remain uncaptured, so only the URL shape this repo builds (`/a/apps/{workspace}/conversations/{id}`) is accepted; other inbox shapes and regional hosts are deliberately not guessed at, so an Intercom + Linear customer may still see low link coverage until captured shapes are added; (2) the L2 replay (Zendesk tenants' `certain` link counts and legs identical) needs the EC2 scratch DB. **Closure (Rev 16, 2026-10-12, documentation only).** (1) **Live capture, owner-confirmed:** the owner confirmed that the live Intercom link capture was already completed. This is the owner's confirmation, not an independently recorded result: the repository holds no record of the date, the link shape(s) captured or the redacted URL, and none is invented here. Nothing was captured or re-run for this closure. The recognition code and its tests are unchanged, so `recognizeIntercomConversationUrl` still accepts only the URL shape this repo builds and no captured shape has been added as a fixture; that describes the current code and is not an open requirement. The 2026-10-05 Jira-side capture above remains independently recorded. (2) **L2 replay, recorded:** 0 differences over 7,413 records on the EC2 host on 2026-10-01 (Rev 6 reconciliation above) and again twice on a local restore on 2026-10-10 (0 differences, 7,413 records, normalized event ids identical; `docs/validation/server-validation-master.md` C-06, owner-recorded). **Behaviour notes:** an org whose Zendesk integration exists but has no subdomain used to return all-zero counters and now counts its links as unrecognized (and runs the unlink sweep); a missing ticket-source integration still returns all zeros.
- [x] **N1.14** `CustomerIdentity` table (expand + dual-write). **Code done 2026-09-30; closes with the final N1 replay on the EC2 scratch DB.** Migration `20260930150000_customer_identity`: table `customer_identities` (`provider`, `kind`, `externalId`, unique on `(organizationId, provider, kind, externalId)`, cascade on the customer) plus a backfill of one row per non-null `zendeskOrgId` (`zendesk`/`organization`), `intercomCompanyId` (`intercom`/`company`) and `intercomContactId` (`intercom`/`contact`). `@sla/db` exports `findCustomerByIdentity` and `upsertCustomerByIdentity`: both resolve through the identity row, fall back to the legacy column (a customer written by rolled-back code is adopted, its identity row created), and the upsert dual-writes the legacy column and survives a concurrent-create race (P2002 re-read). Writers moved: `zendesk/normalize.ts` (organization upsert and the ticket's customer lookup), `intercom/normalize.ts` (company, company lookup, contact fallback); the adapters own the `(provider, kind)` names (`customer-identity.ts` in each). `zendesk/policies.ts` now builds its org→customer map from `CustomerIdentity`. Legacy columns and their three unique keys are untouched until N2.10. Tenant isolation: `tenant-isolation.test.ts` seeds an identity per org and asserts an identity resolves only inside its organization and that the same provider id in two organizations names two customers; `tenant-scope-classification.test.ts` classifies `CustomerIdentity` as direct. Verified locally: `@sla/db validate`; migration on `sla_test` and on a copy of the local dev DB gives 124 identity rows for 124 non-null legacy values; a full Zendesk re-normalization of that copy (1,519 cases, 12 Zendesk integrations) changes **0** cases, and customers, identities and normalized events are unchanged (an L2-style check on dev data, not on the production backup). **Deploy note:** a code rollback leaves customers created afterwards without identity rows; the fallback adopts them on the next normalization, but `zendesk/policies.ts` reads identities only, so run a normalization pass before the next policy import.
- [x] **N1.15** Source-aware Case uniqueness (expand). **Code done 2026-09-30; closes with the final N1 replay on the EC2 scratch DB.** Migration `20260930160000_case_source_integration`: nullable `cases.sourceIntegrationId` (FK to `integrations`, `ON DELETE SET NULL`), backfilled from `(organizationId, system)`, a unique index on `(organizationId, sourceIntegrationId, externalId)`, and `system` loses its `zendesk` default. The old `(organizationId, externalId)` key stays until N2.10. Writers and lookups moved to the compound key: `zendesk/normalize.ts` and `intercom/normalize.ts` (upsert; both also set `sourceIntegrationId` on update), `zendesk/correlate.ts` (official-link correlation), and `buildCaseRefResolver` (the `Case.system` check is gone; the key carries the source). The precondition needs no query (the old key already guarantees no duplicates); the column is nullable only so rows from rolled-back code stay valid, and the migration's backfill `UPDATE` is idempotent, so re-run it to repair them. Removing the default required `system: "zendesk"` on ~30 test fixtures (found by type-check) and one worker DB suite. Verified locally: backfill SQL gives the right integration for zendesk and intercom cases and leaves a `github`-system case null; on the dev-DB copy all 1,519 cases got a `sourceIntegrationId` (0 null) and re-normalization changed none; the zendesk incremental suite asserts a case names its integration; `tracker-link-resolution.test.ts` now proves Zendesk id 77 cannot land on an Intercom case with id 77 through the key alone. Full suite green (201 files, 2,154 tests).
- [x] **N1.16** Intercom as an onboarding ticket source. **Code done 2026-09-30; closes with the final N1 replay.** `OnboardingStatus` gains `intercom`, `linear` and `intercomConfig`; `ticketsFetched` counts Zendesk tickets plus Intercom conversations; `escalatedCases`/`linkedIssues` count Jira and Linear links. `lib/onboarding-progress.ts` (`deriveOnboardingProgress`) is the single definition: step 1 is "a ticket source (Zendesk or Intercom) is connected and its backfill is complete", step 3 is "Jira or Linear is connected"; the activation guard and the flow both use it. `OnboardingFlow`: the static Intercom placeholder is now the real config-gated connect card (Beta badge kept, OAuth returns to `/onboarding?connected=intercom` through a signed `returnTo` in the OAuth state; the settings connect is unchanged), Linear completes step 3 like Jira, GitHub stays optional, the reauth, progress, ledger, activation and dashboard copy name the connected providers, and the stepper reads "Connect Helpdesk" / "Connect Tracker". Intercom has no importable policies (D9), so its review step is a non-blocking "Create your first policy" link to `/settings/sla/configuration` (Zendesk still requires the review). `projectAndEvaluateSourceSyncs` now runs Intercom normalization and Linear correlation/normalization (ticket sources first, then trackers), defers evaluation while any connected source's first backfill is outstanding, and the Intercom and Linear backfill routes call it (Linear's route previously stopped before commitments and evaluation). Tests: `onboarding-progress.test.ts` (pure), `onboarding-intercom.test.ts` (real DB: Intercom-only status, projection, deferral). **Browser walk-through** on a throwaway local org with a stubbed Intercom integration: step 1 shows both cards; with Intercom backfilled the org sits on step 3 with the policy banner; with Linear added it reaches activation, which and the dashboard name Intercom and Linear. Not covered: a live Intercom OAuth round trip (no Intercom OAuth app available here).
- [x] **N1.17** 2×2 provider matrix smoke tests. **Code done 2026-09-30.** `apps/web/test/provider-matrix-smoke.test.ts` (registered in `realDatabaseSuites`): one scenario per pair (Zendesk+Jira, Zendesk+Linear, Intercom+Jira, Intercom+Linear). Each seeds the RawEvents a backfill would store through the adapters' own `map*ToRawEvent` mappers, runs `projectAndEvaluateSourceSyncs`, then a breach evaluation at an explicit future `asOf` and notification claiming with Slack mocked, and asserts: `sourceIntegrationId`, the customer resolved through `CustomerIdentity`, a `certain` CaseLink, an `engineering` leg derived from the stored events, First Response and Resolution evaluated, and exactly one claimed alert that a second claim does not repeat. All four pass; breaking the Intercom writer's `sourceIntegrationId` makes both Intercom pairs fail. **Deviation from the plan:** it does not stub `fetch` for the Intercom and Linear legs; it enters at the stored RawEvents (the HTTP clients have their own package tests), because no webhook route exists for either and driving four provider clients through stubbed HTTP would test the clients, not the boundary. Passing this promotes no pair to production (D17).
- [x] **N1.18** Close-out: final replay; Appendix D invariants updated. **Verification done locally 2026-09-30; the production-backup replay is not done.** §10 passes locally: `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile`, `@sla/db generate` and `validate`, workspace type-check, the provider-literal `grep` (prints nothing), the boundary and golden suites, the golden/e2e-matrix suites, `provider-matrix-smoke` and `tenant-isolation`, and the full suite against `sla_test` (201 files, 2,154 tests). Boundary allowlist: empty (nothing is N2-deferred there). L1 replay on the **local dev DB** (3,914 commitments, 1,508 cases, 13 organizations, `--allow-non-scratch`), baseline code = `7f61ff4` (stashed working tree) vs. this branch's working tree, `--as-of 2026-09-30T09:00:00Z`: **0 differences over 5,422 records, byte-identical captures**; class C drift unchanged (0 status, 1 `breachedAt`). That is not the N1.2 baseline and not the production backup. **Still open, needs the EC2 host:** restore a fresh backup with `scripts/restore-drill.sh`, run `migrate deploy` (all N1 migrations, including the NOT NULL `sourceRole` one in its deploy order), then L1 (`replay:capture` at `2026-09-29T21:36:44Z`, `replay:compare` against the N1.2 baseline, expected 0 class-A) and L2 (re-normalize and correlate; `Case.customerId`, `certain` link counts and legs identical; `CustomerIdentity` count equal to the non-null legacy columns; every case with a `sourceIntegrationId`). Only then can N1.6–N1.18 be ticked and the phase closed. No class-B difference has arisen anywhere in N1 (§13 has no entries), so `docs/customer-guide.md` needs no change.

### Phase N2 — Provider contract + shared projector

**Status:** 🟡 9 of 11 done, merged to `main` (PR #35, 2026-10-01); open: N2.10 (held out of the tree, awaiting a release decision), N2.11 (close-out) · **Estimate:** 4–5 weeks · **Priority:** P0 · **Design record:** [N2 contract architecture](#n2-contract-architecture-as-built-and-the-n210-release) · **Needs:** N1 · **Branch:** `phase/n2-provider-contract-and-projector`
**Goal:** every provider is reached through one statically typed adapter record; adapters return canonical records; one projector persists them.

> **Rev 6 reconciliation (2026-10-05).** `phase/n2-provider-contract-and-projector` is merged into `main`. N2.4 is ticked: its only caveat was that the perf numbers were not production-host numbers. N2.7 is ticked: its one exception, the onboarding wizard, was rebuilt from adapter capabilities in N5.1 and the boundary test now has no onboarding entries. **Still genuinely open:** N2.6 (Zendesk vs Intercom screenshot comparison, never run); **N2.10**, whose migration, `rollback.sql` and `schema.patch` live in `packages/db/prisma/contract/20261001110000_contract_customer_identity_and_case_source/`, outside `prisma/migrations`, so `schema.prisma` still has the legacy `Customer` identity columns, the source-less Case key and a nullable `sourceIntegrationId`; its replay precondition was met on 2026-10-01, and it ships as its own release after a fresh backup; **N2.11**, which closes when N2.6 and N2.10 do. N2.10's NOT NULL migration is not applied anywhere that this repo can show.
> **Rev 7 (2026-10-05).** N2.6 is closed (see its entry). N2.10 was re-verified against the current schema on a restore of the production backup and with the full suite on the contracted schema (see its entry), but stays open: it is applied nowhere and ships as its own release after a fresh backup, at the owner's decision. N2.11 closes with N2.10.
> **Phase is done when:**

- the worker dispatches through a typed registry with no provider branches;
- no provider package writes domain tables;
- web code has no provider branches outside integration/OAuth/webhook/concierge surfaces;
- legacy identity columns and keys are dropped;
- replay is clean and all four pairs pass.

- [x] **N2.1** `@sla/ingestion` package: contract types and shared errors. **Done 2026-10-01.** New `packages/ingestion` with `ProviderAdapter`, `ProviderCapabilities`, `CanonicalBatch`/`LinkFact`, the context types and `ProviderWebAdapter` (types only; shapes follow the existing `run*` functions), plus `ReauthRequiredError`, `PermissionDeniedError` and `ProviderUnavailableError`. The five `*ReauthRequiredError` classes now extend the shared one. **Deviation from the plan:** the `*PermissionDeniedError` classes cannot also extend a shared class, because they must stay a `*ApiError` (`zendesk-concierge-export.ts` checks `instanceof ZendeskApiError && status === 403`). They carry a brand symbol instead, and `error instanceof PermissionDeniedError` matches the brand (`Symbol.hasInstance`). Class names and `status` are unchanged. Verified: workspace type-check; unit project (159 files, 1,899 tests); `apps/worker/test/shared-provider-errors.test.ts` asserts all ten classes against the shared types. Contract members that nothing consumes yet (`importPolicies`/`importCalendars` result shapes, `ConversationMessage`, `CorrelateContext`) are provisional and get finalised in N2.3/N2.5/N2.6/N2.8.
- [x] **N2.2** Shared projector (cases, customer identities, normalized events, links). **Done 2026-10-01.** `projectCanonicalBatch` (customers, cases, soft-deletes, per-record event reconciliation with per-record failure isolation), `projectLinkFacts` and `projectLinkSweep` in `@sla/ingestion`, plus the generalised `diffNormalizedEvents`. Reading the five adapters changed the contract sketched in the plan: (1) trackers and code hosts own no cases, so `CanonicalBatch` carries `eventGroups` aimed at a case by external id (ticket sources) or by `caseId` (trackers, from their `CaseLink`s), each scoped by `ownRawEventIds`, which is what keeps one provider from deleting another's events on a shared case; (2) a group can carry a `linkEvidencePatch` (Jira's `statusName`), applied in the same transaction; (3) `LinkFact` carries the resolved `caseId`, `system`, a merge/replace evidence mode, the first-sighting and re-link event sources, and per-producer method rules, because Jira and Zendesk co-evidence one `CaseLink` and Linear and GitHub do not; (4) the two unlink sweeps (`sweepUnlinkedRemoteLinks` in Jira, `sweepUnlinkedOfficialLinks` in Zendesk) are one `LinkSweep` parameterised by evidence key. The legacy-column dual-write for `Customer` lives in the projector (one mapping to delete in N2.10). **Known behaviour changes to confirm in L2 replay (N2.3):** trackers' events are now diffed, not deleted and recreated, so unchanged events keep their id and `createdAt` (the worker's new-events lookback no longer re-selects those cases every cycle); the `repairLinkedEvent` flag keeps today's difference between Jira/Zendesk (events only on create or re-link) and Linear/GitHub (a missing event is repaired). **Not yet possible:** two ticket sources reusing one external id inside an organization still collide on the legacy `(organizationId, externalId)` key; the projector reports it as a failure. Add that case to `projector.test.ts` in N2.10. Verified: `apps/web/test/projector.test.ts` (22 tests, real Postgres) and `packages/ingestion` unit tests; workspace type-check; full suite against `sla_test` (215 files, 2,309 tests).
- [x] **N2.3** Adapters migrated to canonical batches (Intercom → Linear → Jira → GitHub → Zendesk). **Code done 2026-10-01; L2 on the dev DB clean, production-backup L2 still open (N2.11).** Each provider package now exports pure builders (`buildIntercomBatch`, `buildLinearBatch`, `buildJiraBatch`, `buildGithubBatch`, `buildZendeskBatch`) and correlators returning `CorrelationOutput` (`correlateJira`, `correlateLinear`, `correlateGithub`, `correlateZendeskJiraLinks`), plus an `adapter.ts` with its `ProviderAdapter` (and `ProviderWebAdapter`) record. `run*Normalization` and `run*Correlation` are gone; no provider package writes `Case`, `Customer`, `CustomerIdentity`, `CaseLink` or `NormalizedEvent` (the boundary test enforces it). Zendesk's watermark stays in its adapter and advances through `CanonicalBatch.afterProject`, after the projector has written. **Deviations from the plan:** (1) not one commit per provider: the work is one working tree, uncommitted, at the owner's instruction, so there is no per-provider revert point; (2) the adapter record lives in each provider package and the registries are one-line-per-provider literals, instead of the worker composing the record; (3) contract members finalised: `recognizeCaseUrl(url, credentials)`, `IngestResult {recordsFetched, counts}`, `CorrelateContext.resolveCaseRef: CaseRefResolver | null` and `externalIds`, `CorrelationOutput.evaluated/unmatched`, `CanonicalBatch.afterProject`, `ImportContext.ensureDefaultCalendarVersion`, import result shapes, and a fourth shared error, `IntegrationNotConfiguredError`; (4) **ticket deletions are now two steps**: ingestion records a content-hashed `ticket_deleted:{id}:{hash}` raw event (backfill's `deleted` status and 404-on-audits, and the webhook's 404), and normalization emits `deletedCaseExternalIds`, so the Case soft-delete lands at normalization, in the same cycle, instead of inside backfill; the Zendesk webhook route normalizes the deleted ticket right away. Jira's issue-deleted handling is `recordJiraIssueDeletion` (raw event) plus `projectIssueRemoval`. **Behaviour changes, confirmed on the dev DB:** trackers' and Intercom's events are diffed, not deleted and recreated, so unchanged events keep their id and `createdAt`. L2-style check on a copy of the local dev DB (13 organizations, 1,527 cases, 12 Zendesk / 12 Jira / 1 Linear integrations; Intercom and GitHub are connected but hold no cases there): fingerprint before, apply both N2 migrations, re-normalize and correlate every integration through the new registry, fingerprint after. **0 differences over 7,409 records** (cases, events by content hash, links with evidence hash, customers and identities, commitments), **0 record failures, and the set of 9,735 `NormalizedEvent` ids identical**. Not covered by this check: Intercom and GitHub on real data (they are pinned by `intercom-normalization.test.ts` and the matrix smoke test), and the production backup.
- [x] **N2.4** Worker registry and role-ordered dispatch. **Done 2026-10-01.** `apps/worker/src/providers.ts` (`PROVIDERS … satisfies Record<IntegrationProvider, ProviderAdapter>`); `cycle.ts` has no `provider ===` branch: ingest, correlate, normalize and the Zendesk imports run through `ingest` / `syncIntegration`, and the three shared error types (plus `IntegrationNotConfiguredError`) drive the status transitions, which are unchanged. Integrations are ordered ticket source → work tracker → code host, replacing `PROVIDER_CYCLE_PRIORITY` (which only put GitHub last); a ticket source normalizes before it correlates, a tracker correlates before it normalizes. New `registry-dispatch.test.ts` (five stub adapters: order, one `ProviderUnavailableError` leaves the others ingesting and evaluation running, reauth/permission from the shared types); `cycle.test.ts` now stubs Linear's correlate/normalize through the registry. **Perf baseline (2026-10-01, local, same machine, same data):** a 5,000-case / 200,346-event organization seeded in `sla_test` (`seed-perf-baseline`, 77 s), `PERF_METRICS=1 PERF_ORG_ID=<org> perf:baseline` run alternately on `HEAD` (a clean worktree) and the N2 tree, 9 rounds each. Stage work is unchanged (queries per stage identical; `worker_normalize` has one extra query, +20–90 ms of a 0.3–0.4 s stage). On the quiet runs: poll tick 4.1–5.2 s old vs 4.1–5.5 s new, sweep 4.0–5.5 s vs 4.2–4.7 s. The machine then became noisy and both sides spiked (old up to 18 s, new up to 18 s), so the medians of the noisy rounds are not evidence either way; no regression is distinguishable, and the figures are in line with or better than the recorded 2026-09-29 numbers (`docs/capacity-limits.md`: 5,000 cases, poll lock held 6.9 s). Not production-host numbers.
- [x] **N2.5** Link resolution through the registry. **Done 2026-10-01.** `ticketUrlRecognizers(PROVIDERS)` replaces both two-entry maps (worker and web `case-ref.ts`); trackers' and Zendesk's official-link correlation return `LinkFact`s and sweeps that `correlateAndProject` persists. Verified: `provider-matrix-smoke` (4 of 4 pairs), `official-link-correlation`, `jira-remote-link-unlink`, `tracker-link-resolution`, and 0 link differences in the dev-DB check above. The GitHub backfill route now projects under the organization lock (it did not before).
- [x] **N2.6** Web registry: external URLs and Conversation rendering through adapters. **Done 2026-10-01.** `apps/web/src/lib/providers.ts` (`PROVIDERS`, `WEB_PROVIDERS`, role helpers). Zendesk and Intercom conversation parsing moved into `renderZendeskConversation` / `renderIntercomConversation`; `case-detail-data.ts` loads the raw events and calls the source adapter's `renderConversation` (Conversation stays message-only). `externalUrl` replaces the URL builders in case detail and the report; `ISSUE_TRACKER_SYSTEMS` and the `["jira","linear","github"]` lists became `isIssueLinkSystem` / `ISSUE_LINK_PROVIDERS`. Case detail now reads the organization's integrations in one query. **Compliance report CSV is unchanged on purpose:** its provider-specific headers ("Zendesk URL", "Jira issues", …) are customer-facing, so they are fixed in `lib/providers.ts` (`REPORT_*`) rather than derived; as before, only Zendesk cases get a ticket URL (a known gap). `CaseLinkDetail.system` no longer includes `zendesk`, which never occurred. **Browser comparison done 2026-10-05 (Rev 7), on fixtures, not a real Intercom workspace.** A throwaway local org with one Zendesk ticket and one Intercom conversation of the same shape (stored through the adapters' own `map*ToRawEvent` mappers, projected with `projectAndEvaluateSourceSyncs`, a Jira link on each; deleted afterwards) was walked in the browser, case list and case detail. Commitments, legs, link and timeline rendered the same for both. It found and fixed four differences that were provider leaks, not data: (1) the case header keyed every case `ZD-<id>`, so an Intercom case read `ZD-7001` (now `formatCaseKey`: `ZD-` for Zendesk, `IC-` for Intercom); (2) the case list said `Zendesk #<id>` and `ZD: Open` for every source (now the case's own source, `CaseListRow.system`); (3) three static subtitles named Zendesk and Jira (now neutral); (4) the Intercom conversation omitted the customer's opening message that Zendesk's shows (Intercom keeps it in the conversation's `source`, not as a part; `intercomWebAdapter.conversationContext` now hands it to `renderIntercomConversation`; display only, it never becomes a reply event). Left as they are: Zendesk shows agents as "Agent" because only a comment author's role is stored, while Intercom shows the stored name; the compliance report's ticket-URL column is still Zendesk-only (above); and the case list shows a Jira key under an `ENG-` prefix (`ENG-KAN-1`), which predates this task and was not changed. Tests: `packages/intercom/test/conversation.test.ts`, an Intercom case in `case-detail-conversation.test.ts`, `formatCaseKey` and `formatTicketSourceShort` in `format.test.ts`; rendering is otherwise covered by `providers.test.ts`. **Not done, stated plainly:** the removed plan 02's wording asked for "identical rendering before and after" the refactor with screenshots; no pre-N2 build was run for that. The refactor's before/after is pinned by tests instead: `case-detail-conversation.test.ts`'s Zendesk assertions are identical before and after the N2 refactor (only the integration fake changed, `git diff c50c4ff^1 c50c4ff`) and pass.
- [x] **N2.7** Capability-driven connect-time projection, webhooks and onboarding. **Done 2026-10-01, onboarding excepted.** `source-sync.ts` iterates the connected ticket sources and trackers through the registry (`syncIntegration`); imports run only when the capability is set. The result is now `{ providers: { <provider>: { normalization, correlation, calendarImport, policyImport } }, … }` and the backfill routes map it back to their existing response keys. The webhook routes keep their provider-specific ingest and use `verifyWebhook` and `normalizeAndProject` / `syncIntegration`; the integration detail page reads `webhooks` from the capability. **Carry-over to N5.1 (planned, not a defect):** the onboarding wizard (`modules/onboarding`, `lib/onboarding-*`, `lib/types/onboarding.ts`, `actions/onboarding.ts`) is deliberately still built around two ticket sources and two trackers; making its steps capability-driven is N5.1's redesign. N2 does not touch it. It is exempted in the boundary test by path, with that reason, instead of being allow-listed, and N5.1 removes the exemption (the boundary test then has no onboarding paths).
- [x] **N2.8** Import summary generalised per provider. **Done 2026-10-01.** Migration `20261001100000_sla_import_summary_provider` (nullable `provider`, backfilled `zendesk`); `recordSlaImportSummary` takes the provider; `getPolicyImportReview` reads the summary of a provider with the `policyImport` capability. Counter names unchanged.
- [x] **N2.9** Boundary test extended to the worker and web. **Done 2026-10-01.** Four new rules in `provider-boundary.test.ts`: no `provider === "…"` in `apps/worker/src` outside `providers.ts`; provider-name literals in `apps/web/src` only in the plan's list plus the integration-settings read models (`lib/integrations-data.ts`, `lib/integration-detail-data.ts`, `lib/types/integrations.ts`) and the onboarding wizard (see N2.7); provider packages import only types and error classes from `@sla/ingestion`; no provider package writes a domain table. The ratchet allowlist is empty.
- [~] **N2.10** Contract migrations: drop legacy `Customer` identity columns and the source-less Case key. **Code and migration done 2026-10-01; do not deploy yet.** Migration `20261001110000_contract_customer_identity_and_case_source` re-runs both expand backfills idempotently, aborts if any case still has no source integration, drops the three `Customer` columns and their keys and `cases_organizationId_externalId_key`, makes `sourceIntegrationId` NOT NULL (FK `ON DELETE NO ACTION`, so an organization delete can cascade in either order), and ships `rollback.sql` beside it (re-add and repopulate from `CustomerIdentity`). The dual-write and the legacy fallback are removed from `@sla/db` and the projector. **Held out of the tree on purpose (2026-10-01, at the owner's instruction):** the migration, `rollback.sql`, a `schema.patch` for `schema.prisma` and a README now live in `packages/db/prisma/contract/20261001110000_…/`, outside `prisma/migrations`, so `migrate deploy` cannot apply it; `schema.prisma` is HEAD plus N2.8 only. It had been applied earlier to `sla_test` and to a throwaway copy of the dev DB; `sla_test` was reverted with `rollback.sql` and its `_prisma_migrations` row deleted, the copy was dropped, and the code was re-verified against the pre-N2.10 schema. **Precondition not met:** the plan needs one production release on dual-write and a clean production-backup L2 (N1.18's EC2 replay is still open). Ship this migration as its own release, after a verified backup; the N2.3–N2.9 code runs against either schema. `pnpm --filter @sla/db validate` passes. **Re-verified 2026-10-05 (Rev 7), still applied nowhere:** on a local restore of the 2026-10-02 production backup (1,528 cases, 12 organizations) after `migrate deploy` of all later migrations, the preconditions hold (0 cases without a source integration, 124 identities = 124 legacy values, 0 cross-source duplicate ids), the migration applies in one transaction, and `rollback.sql` restores the three legacy columns with an identical checksum of their values. With `schema.patch` applied (it still applies to current `main`), `prisma validate` passes, `prisma migrate diff` between the contracted database and the patched schema reports no difference, `pnpm type-check` is clean and the **full suite passes (256 files, 2,755 tests)** against the contracted schema (isolated worktree and database; the dev stack and `main` were not touched). Open for the release only: a fresh production backup, the owner's go-ahead and the host replay after it.
- [~] **N2.11** Close-out. **Local verification done 2026-10-01; the phase is not closed.** Passing locally: `@sla/db generate` and `validate`, `pnpm type-check`, `pnpm build`, `next build`, the provider-literal `grep` (prints nothing), the boundary allowlist (empty), `provider-matrix-smoke` (4 of 4), golden, e2e-matrix and tenant-isolation, and the full suite against `sla_test`. Flaky tests fixed 2026-10-01: `seed-test-customers.test.ts` builds whole datasets and timed out at the 5 s default under the parallel full run (explicit 60 s budget on its three describes); `seeded-tenant-isolation.test.ts` called `getCaseListData` without the fixed `now`, so wall-clock-derived numbers could collide with another tenant's ticket id (it now passes `now`). **Production-backup replay, run 2026-10-01 on the EC2 host** (the production host, repo at `~/elapsed`, production at `7cb2b9b`, which already contains N1 and its migrations through `20260930170000`). Fresh read-only `pg_dump` of the live `elapsed_db` (`sla-20261001T104033Z.dump`, 4.5 MB; **note `backup.sh` dumps `$POSTGRES_DB`, which is the empty `sla_breach_monitoring` here, so it needs `DB_NAME=elapsed_db`**), restored into `sla_restore_drill` (1,528 cases, 124 customers, 124 identities, 12 organizations). Baseline with the deployed image's own code, then `migrate deploy` with the N2 code (applied **only** `20261001100000_sla_import_summary_provider`; N2.10 absent), then: **L2 0 differences over 7,413 records, 0 record failures, all 9,743 `NormalizedEvent` ids identical, a second run also 0; L1 baseline vs N2 code 0 differences over 5,440 records (class A unapproved 0, class B 0)**; class C drift unchanged at 0 status / 9 `breachedAt` of 3,923 commitments (N1.2: 0 / 10). ### Phase N3 — Provider isolation + freshness

**Status:** 🟡 8 of 9 done (7 on `main` since 2026-10-02, commit `eee09a7`, no separate phase branch; **N3.6 closed 2026-10-11 with the two-hour drill WAIVED, not passed**; its W1 and W2 tests are on `testing`, commit `26038d1`). Open: N3.7 (evidence-gated) · **Estimate:** 2–3 weeks · **Priority:** P0 · **Design record:** [N3 freshness rules](#n3-freshness-rules-and-the-n37-circuit-breaker-design) · **Needs:** N2, D13 (for N3.5) · **Branch:** none; landed on `main` directly
**Goal:** a provider outage degrades only its own integrations and tenants, and stale data is labelled, not silent.
**Phase is done when:**

- **(Rev 11, owner-approved; the original wording, "in a simulated 2-hour outage, other tenants' tick time is within ±10%", is WAIVED, not passed.)** In the short automated outage test (N3.6, W2), the healthy organizations' median per-organization run duration during one organization's outage is within ±10% of the control phase of the same test, and no stale-source alert goes out without D13 treatment (the existing real-DB coverage, not repeated);
- freshness is visible to customers and operators;
- the circuit breaker is either built with trigger evidence or recorded as not needed (D22).

- [x] **N3.1** Last successful sync, consecutive failures and duration per integration. **Code and additive migration written 2026-10-01, not deployed; verification closed 2026-10-05 (Rev 7).** The backfill was checked on a local restore of the 2026-10-02 production backup after `migrate deploy`: of 26 integrations, the 4 with no `lastSyncError` carry `lastSuccessfulSyncAt = lastSyncAt` and the 22 with one carry none (failures are left unknown on purpose); no integration has a failure streak. New real-Postgres worker test `apps/worker/test/integration-sync-health.db.test.ts` drives the real cycle through success, failure, failure, success: the last success stays put, `consecutiveFailures` counts, `failingSince` marks where the outage began and does not move on the second failure, and recovery clears both (mutation-tested). The Zendesk and Jira webhook route tests now assert that a processed delivery sets `lastSuccessfulSyncAt` and clears the streak.
- [x] **N3.2** Pure freshness function in `@sla/core`. **Done 2026-10-01; unit-tested.**
- [x] **N3.3** Freshness recorded on evaluations (metadata only; replay 0 differences when fresh). **Code and migration written 2026-10-01, not deployed; replay closed 2026-10-05 (Rev 7).** L1 replay on a local restore of the 2026-10-02 production backup (3,923 commitments, 1,517 cases, 12 organizations, `--as-of 2026-10-02T03:38:46Z`): a baseline with the pre-N3 code (`c50c4ff`, the N2 merge) against `main` after `migrate deploy` of all seven pending migrations gives **0 differences over 5,440 records (class A unapproved 0, class B 0)**, class C drift unchanged (0 status, 9 `breachedAt`). `stale-source-notifications.test.ts` shows a stale source sets `sourceStaleSince` and leaves the evaluation's status untouched. Run on a restore, not on the production host.
- [x] **N3.4** Decide D13. **Done: D13(b), already recorded above.**
- [x] **N3.5** Freshness-aware notifications, per D13. **D13(b) implemented: stale at-risk alerts are caveated and stale breach alerts are held; dedicated real-DB test added 2026-10-01 (`stale-source-notifications.test.ts`: stale at-risk labelled, stale breach held, held breach sent exactly once after recovery).**
- [x] **N3.6** Per-request timeouts, tick/integration durations, outage drill test. **30-second per-attempt timeout and structured per-integration duration are implemented 2026-10-01; outage drill added 2026-10-01 (`cycle.test.ts`: three organizations, a Linear stub, one organization delayed 400 ms and then answered with HTTP 400; it is not a true hang); per-integration `lastSyncDurationMs` is persisted. Work is per-organization now, so there is no global tick to persist. **Rev 11 (2026-10-11): the two-hour production-scale drill is permanently WAIVED by the owner (waived, never passed). Closed 2026-10-11 with the two-hour drill WAIVED (not passed): the two short tests below (W1, W2) pass and are committed on `testing` (`26038d1`).\*\*
  - **Rev 11 closure (2026-10-11, owner decisions; supersedes the Rev 10 block below wherever they conflict).**
    - **Waiver (permanent).** The two-hour production-scale outage drill is **waived by the owner** (2026-10-11, in chat). It is recorded as **WAIVED, never passed**: the "2-hour" clause of this task's acceptance criteria, of the former plan 03 acceptance criterion 1 and of the N3 done-when bullet 1 is unmet by design and will not be scheduled, run or proposed again. No supervised or multi-hour validation is required to close N3.6.
    - **Replacement validation (short, automated, no supervision, existing test infrastructure only; no new harness).** **W1:** a focused `@sla/http-retry` unit test of the per-attempt timeout (`packages/http-retry/test/retry.test.ts`). **W2:** the existing outage test in `apps/worker/test/cycle.test.ts` ("provider outage drill (N3.6)"), extended to one failing organization that truly hangs and six healthy organizations at concurrency 3 (so the healthy ones must share the remaining slots), run as a control phase (no outage) and an outage phase in the same test. Both go on the `testing` branch (CLAUDE.md). The D13 alert evidence is **not repeated**: stale at-risk labelling, held breach, and exactly one send after recovery stay covered by `apps/web/test/stale-source-notifications.test.ts` (real DB; passed in ledger B-07 and the E-02 full run).
    - **±10% criterion (defined).** A **same-test comparison between healthy organizations during the control phase and the outage phase**: the median of the healthy organizations' per-organization run duration (`organization_finished.durationMs`) during the outage phase must be within ±10% of their median in the control phase of the same test (same organizations, same stubbed latency). A historical pre-outage median is not used: in the 4h41m quiet worker log (`~/elapsed-validation/worker.log`, no outage) the 30-minute window medians moved 13% to 28% peak to peak. Because millisecond-scale medians are noise-dominated, the comparison passes if the difference is within 10% of the control median **or within an absolute floor of 15 ms, whichever is larger** (the floor comes from the owner-approved decision OD-B; the value is recorded here so it is not silent). Because a run's own duration excludes time spent queued for a slot, W2 additionally asserts that every healthy organization finishes while the failing organization is still hung.
    - **25% criterion (defined).** The 25% of the active-poll interval threshold applies to **healthy organizations only**: a healthy organization's per-organization run duration must stay at or below 25% of the active-poll interval (production 30 s, so 7.5 s). The failing organization is excluded by definition: one 30 s attempt is already 100% of the interval and the worst case for one request is about 210 s. At stub scale W2's check is a regression guard; the real-world headroom is the baseline, not an outage measurement: 0 of 1,660 runs in the quiet 4h41m log exceeded 7.5 s, but the maximum was 6,972 ms (23.2%), so it is close to the bound. The N3.7 trigger wording (N3 design record) is read the same way, and **N3.7 keeps its D22 gate** (C-18 needs at least 3 weeks of data, only Week 1 exists; the W2 result replaces the drill result as the other input; the owner decides).
    - **Approved duration measurements.** `organization_finished` (structured log: `durationMs`, `ingestMs`, `pipelineMs`; per organization, not persisted) and `Integration.lastSyncDurationMs` (persisted per integration, shown in the operator view). **`WorkerSettings.lastActivePollDurationMs` and `lastReconciliationDurationMs` are not written and not used for this validation** (the columns stay in the schema and migration `20261001120000_provider_freshness`; no migration, no writers). A search of `apps` and `packages` (excluding generated code and documentation) finds no reader either, so no code or alert depends on them: the worker-stall alert is heartbeat-based and unchanged.
    - **Dependent definitions that need rebasing (identified, not changed here):** the Validation Metrics row "Worker active-poll tick p95 ÷ interval"; the **N8-S1** trigger "tick p95 > 50% of the interval" (its copy in the removed plan 08 is now in the N8 design record, S1); ledger C-18 (its SQL, the N8-S1 evaluation rule and the Week 1 "tick ratio" line); the N3 design record and N3.7; and the removed plan 04's operator-view line "that org's tick duration (N3)" (no longer a file). Each carried a pointer note (the removed plan files' notes were migrated in Rev 14); the N8 trigger definition is not decided here.
    - **Accepted residual risks.** (1) Nothing was measured over two continuous hours: growth of sync-run rows and logs over such a window is not measured (at a 30 s poll, about 240 `IntegrationSyncRun` rows per integration, with the existing 30-day and 14-day retention); the reasoning that outage length is not a variable (constant-time freshness check, bounded counters, one lease and one slot per hung organization, a held breach alert does not consume its claim) is argued from the code, not measured. (2) W2 uses a Linear stub and an in-memory database, not a real provider, hardware, PostgreSQL or a multi-worker process: real-process contention under an outage is not measured. (3) The ±10% check uses a same-test control and a 15 ms floor, so a smaller regression than the floor at stub scale is not detected. (4) The 25% bound is a guard at test scale; the near-bound baseline maximum (23.2%) is a watch item. (5) The failing organization's own run exceeds 25% by configuration; that is accepted, not measured. (6) Production scale is not defined: the owner-reported production holds 1 organization and 24 cases (ledger A-09).
    - **Corrections to earlier claims.** (a) _Harness:_ BL-05 and D-02 said no outage-injection harness exists. A partial one does: `packages/custom-ticket/dev/mock-helpdesk.mjs` (`POST /__control` modes `error500`, `hang`, `slow`, `forbidden`, `badjson`, `redirect`; global per process, custom REST provider only), used in ledger B-11 and B-13 on 2026-10-10 (13 `500` and 6 `hang` requests in `mock.log`); the bench fake (`apps/worker/scripts/bench/fake-linear-preload.mjs`) is latency-only and keyed per organization token. Neither is needed for W1 or W2. (b) _Existing test:_ the drill in `cycle.test.ts` is three organizations (two healthy, below the concurrency of 3), a Linear stub, a 400 ms delay then HTTP 400, no control phase and no duration comparison; it is not a hang and not a 503. The close-out's "real-DB drills pass" refers to `stale-source-notifications.test.ts`, which is a D13 test, not an outage drill. (c) _Production scale:_ the "10 live tenants" shape (`capacity-limits.md`) is a seeded laptop run, not production; the owner-reported production is 1 organization and 24 cases (A-09), which conflicts with the roadmap's "10 customers" (open decision OD-07, not resolved here). (d) _Timeout evidence:_ the live 30 s timeout seen in ledger B-13 is the `safe-http` client's (custom REST), not `@sla/http-retry`'s; W1 covers `@sla/http-retry`.
    - **Results (2026-10-11).** W1 and W2 passed on 2026-10-11 on a worktree of `testing` (`/Users/yasseralnajjar/Workspace/ideas/elapsed-testing-n36`, branch `testing` fast-forwarded to `origin/testing` `7b4ee3f`, whose `apps/worker` and `packages/http-retry` code is identical to `main`), **committed on `testing` as `26038d1` (not pushed)**. W1: `packages/http-retry/test/retry.test.ts`, 5 new tests (30 s default constant, live signal per attempt, a hung attempt aborted with a `TimeoutError`, a timed-out attempt retried with a fresh signal, attempts capped when every attempt hangs). W2: `apps/worker/test/cycle.test.ts`, 4 new tests in "provider outage drill (N3.6)" (control phase; failure confined to the hung organization with every healthy one finishing while it is still hung; ±10%; 25%). Command: `npx vitest run packages/http-retry/test/retry.test.ts apps/worker/test/cycle.test.ts` → 2 files, 41 tests, 0 failed (the two files contained 9 + 23 tests before); `pnpm --filter @sla/http-retry type-check` and `pnpm --filter @sla/worker type-check` clean; six repeats of `-t "N3.6"` (10 tests each) all passed. One observed run (a throwaway probe copy, since deleted): healthy per-organization durations control 109/109/102/103/104/102 ms, outage 102/104/103/102/102/101 ms (both medians 102.5 ms; bound for 25% of 30 s is 7,500 ms). Sensitivity was checked with a throwaway mutation (concurrency 1, so the hung organization blocks the only slot): the outage test then failed (before the guard was tightened it timed out after 5 s; the guard is now 2 s so it fails as an assertion). No database, production or network access was used; `prisma generate` ran with a placeholder `DATABASE_URL` only to resolve the config.
    - **Closure rule.** N3.6 is ticked `[x]` (2026-10-11, on the owner's instruction) after W1 and W2 passed and were committed on `testing` (`26038d1`); the results are recorded here and in ledger D-02. It is closed **with the waiver**, not "passed": the two-hour clause stays recorded as waived, and N3.7 remains open under D22.
  - **Rev 10 reconciliation (2026-10-10, documentation only; no harness built, nothing run). History: its drill, environment, harness and "unresolved owner decisions" items are superseded by the Rev 11 block above.**
    - **Established (repository, per the 2026-10-10 review):** the per-attempt timeout and the per-integration `lastSyncDurationMs` are implemented. Per-organization duration is emitted only as structured log fields (`organization_finished`: `durationMs`, `ingestMs`, `pipelineMs`, in `apps/worker/src/cycle.ts`; `work_processed` and `work_finished`: `durationMs`, in `apps/worker/src/work-loop.ts`) and is not persisted. `WorkerSettings.lastActivePollDurationMs` and `lastReconciliationDurationMs` exist in the schema and migration `20261001120000_provider_freshness` but have **no writers** in the code (a search of `apps` and `packages`, excluding documentation), so those metrics are **not available**. The only drill is a unit test (`apps/worker/test/cycle.test.ts`, "provider outage drill (N3.6)": three organizations, a stubbed 400 ms outage). The fake provider in `apps/worker/scripts/bench/` adds latency only and fakes Linear only; it cannot hang or fail one tenant.
    - **Owner-reported (validation ledger):** C-18 Week 1 (2026-10-10) records the tick ratio as not computable because the two fields are empty. Production host: t3.medium, 2 vCPU, 3.7 GiB, disk 77% of 48 GB, container logs unbounded (A-08, A-10). Production holds 1 organization and 24 cases, connected to Intercom and Jira (A-09). None of this was independently observed by an engineering session.
    - **Proposed, needs owner approval:** the drill runs only in an isolated staging or bench environment, never against a real customer's provider or polling (D13 would hold that customer's breach alerts). The final environment and host are **not decided**. Read-only production monitoring (`/admin/monitoring`, ledger C-18) is a different activity from outage injection and is the only one of the two permitted on production. For the duration criterion, either writers for the two fields are implemented, or an alternative metric is approved (for example per-organization duration read from the `organization_finished` log); until one is approved the criterion and the Validation Metrics row "tick p95 / interval" cannot be measured.
    - **Acceptance (unchanged):** during a 2-hour outage of one tenant's provider, every other tenant's per-organization run duration stays within ±10% of its pre-outage median; per D13(b), the outage tenant's at-risk alerts carry the stale marker and its breach alerts are held, then sent once after recovery. **Not yet measurable:** the ±10% criterion (no approved metric, see above) and the alert behavior at scale (no per-tenant failure-injection harness; existing coverage is the unit drill and `apps/web/test/stale-source-notifications.test.ts`).
    - **Prerequisites and stop conditions:** a per-tenant failure-injection harness (hang and 503; it should cover the providers production uses, which the owner reports as Intercom and Jira, while the existing fake is Linear only); an isolated host or staging environment; a disposable database whose name contains `bench` or `test`, with no real credentials or encryption keys. The production host cannot host the drill: 2 vCPU and 3.7 GiB, and disk about 2.4 GB below the 80% stop line (derived from the owner-reported 77% of 48 GB). Stop if any real provider call is seen, if the target database name lacks `bench` or `test`, if disk reaches 80%, or on memory or swap pressure. Cleanup: drop the scratch database, delete any dump or harness output, and confirm disk is back under 80%.
    - **Unresolved owner decisions:** (1) the final drill environment and host; (2) the target production scale (organization and case counts are **undefined**; none is proposed here); (3) instrument the two fields or approve an alternative metric; (4) N3.7 stays gated by D22: no circuit breaker is built and no "not needed" conclusion is recorded until C-18 has at least 3 weeks of data (only Week 1 exists), the drill result is in, and the owner decides.
- [ ] **N3.7** Circuit breaker, **only if N3.6 justifies it**. **Decision pending production measurements (D22): not built and not to be built yet. The unit drill only shows timeouts plus bounded organization concurrency isolate a hung tenant; build the breaker only if production per-integration durations/failure streaks show it is needed, otherwise record "not needed".** _(Rev 10: the D22 gate is preserved; see the N3.6 reconciliation above. Neither building it nor recording "not needed" is justified before the measurements and the owner's decision. Rev 11: the two-hour drill result is no longer an input; the inputs are C-18 (at least 3 weeks) and the W2 result, and the 25% trigger reading applies to healthy organizations only, as defined in N3.6.)_
- [x] **N3.8** Customer-visible freshness (settings, dashboard, banner, case detail). **Done 2026-10-01 and browser-verified against the local stack (fresh, stale-with-timestamp and never-synced states; thresholds matched worker settings: 30 s × grace 3 = 90 s).**

### Phase N4 — Platform Admin + plan records

**Status:** ✅ 6 of 6 done, merged to `main` (PR #36, 2026-10-02); N4.7 (plan-record data entry) removed by the owner in Rev 15 · **Estimate:** 3 weeks · **Priority:** P1 · **Design record:** [N4 authorization boundaries](#n4-authorization-boundaries) · **Needs:** N3; D14 for plan names · **Branch:** `phase/n4-platform-admin-and-plan-records`
**Goal:** answer "who are the customers, which plan, are they healthy?" on one page, with every operator action audited and nothing platform-level visible to customers.
**Phase is done when:**

- `/admin/tenants` shows plan, status and health for every tenant;
- non-operators get 404/403;
- every admin action is audited.

- [x] **N4.1** Separate `/admin` shell; `/operator` redirects. **Done 2026-10-02, not deployed.** `app/(admin)/admin/` has its own layout (top bar, no tenant sidebar) with `/admin`, `/admin/monitoring`, `/admin/tenants`, `/admin/tenants/[organizationId]`, `/admin/audit`. `requirePlatformAdminPage()` (`lib/admin-auth.ts`) is `notFound()` for anyone who is not on `PLATFORM_ADMIN_EMAILS`; it guards the layout and every `AdminActions` read. `/operator` and `/operator/monitoring` redirect (307, `next.config.mjs`) for one release, then delete that block. The tenant nav lost its operator group: an operator now gets one "Platform admin" link, a non-operator gets none. `modules/operator` became `modules/admin/{overview,monitoring,tenants,tenant-detail,audit}`. **Verified:** `admin-authz.test.ts` (routes found on disk; owner and member get not-found on every read and 403 on every handler, signed-out 401, database never reached; mutation-tested by removing a guard), and live in the browser as a real non-operator org owner: 404 on all five `/admin` pages, 403 on the three admin APIs.
- [x] **N4.2** Append-only admin audit log. **Done 2026-10-02, not deployed.** Migration `20261002100000_platform_admin_and_plan_records` adds `AdminAuditLog` (no foreign keys, so it outlives an organization). `lib/admin-audit.ts` is the only code that touches it and exposes only `recordAdminAudit` (create) and `listAdminAuditLog` (read); `admin-boundary.test.ts` fails if any other file touches the delegate, uses anything but `create`/`findMany`, or if any source or migration updates, deletes or truncates the table in SQL. Every mutation writes its row in the same transaction as the change (a failing audit write rolls the change back, mutation-tested); a no-op or refused request writes none. Actions: `view_tenant`, `update_plan`, `pause_polling`, `resume_polling`, `request_renormalize`, and `update_worker_settings` (the existing worker-settings save is audited too, with before/after). `/admin/audit` is read-only, newest first, cursor-paged. **Known behaviour:** every render of a tenant page writes a `view_tenant` row, so the refresh after a save or pause adds one view row beside the action; a short de-duplication window would quiet it, at the cost of "every view is a row".
- [x] **N4.3** Manual plan and status record per organization (plan names wait on D14). **Done 2026-10-02, not deployed.** `Organization.plan` (string), `planStatus` (enum `trial|active|past_due|cancelled|internal`, default `trial`), `trialEndsAt`, `billingReference`. D14 is decided, so the API accepts only `starter`, `team`, `enterprise` (or empty); the column stays a string for N6. Edited from the tenant page, audited with before/after. Nothing in the worker, evaluation or alerting reads these columns, and the organization settings API neither returns nor writes them (tested). Existing organizations start on `trial` with no plan recorded.
- [x] **N4.4** Tenants list and tenant detail read models, including shared link coverage. **Done 2026-10-02, not deployed.** `lib/admin-tenants-data.ts`: a fixed number of queries however many tenants there are (asserted: identical for 2 and for 8 tenants, and at most 15), evaluation and alert counts through one `GROUP BY` each. List columns as planned, plus a one-word health, plan-status counts, "plan not recorded" count and provider-pair counts (counts only, no names). Detail adds per-integration health with backfill completion from the cursor, recent alert failures (ticket id only, never the subject), `SlaImportSummary`, cases with no matching policy (the dashboard's query) and that organization's own last worker run. `lib/link-coverage-data.ts` is the shared function (two `groupBy` queries; certain, active, tracker-or-code-host links on cases opened in 30 days) for N5 to reuse. **Verified:** real-database tests with two organizations of different sizes (no cross-contamination), no credential or cursor in any read model, and in the browser against seeded tenants. The query-count assertion uses a Prisma `$extends` counter rather than `PERF_METRICS`; `perf:baseline` was not re-run.
- [x] **N4.5** Minimal admin actions: pause/resume polling, request re-normalization. **Done 2026-10-02, not deployed.** `Integration.pollingPausedAt` and `renormalizeRequestedAt`. The worker skips only the provider fetch for a paused integration; normalization and evaluation still run, and no sync health is written, so `lastSuccessfulSyncAt` stops advancing and the integration goes stale (and per D13 breach alerts for its cases are held). A request forces one full normalization pass on the next run and is cleared compare-and-set only after that pass succeeds. Webhook deliveries are not paused: this is polling only. Customers see "Syncing for X is paused by Elapsed support" on the integration list card and detail page. Confirmed dialogs, scoped to one integration, audited. **Verified:** worker tests (paused integration not ingested but normalized, no health written, request forces full then clears, failed pass keeps it, works while paused), real-database action and route tests, and in the browser.
- [x] **N4.6** Boundary guarantees: admin modules unreachable from tenant code. **Done 2026-10-02, not deployed.** `apps/web/test/admin-boundary.test.ts`: `lib/admin-*`, `actions/admin*.ts`, `components/admin/*` and `modules/admin/*` are imported only from the admin area; the tenant `Actions` barrel no longer includes any admin read; no tenant file mentions the admin or worker-settings URLs (`WorkerSettings.save` moved to `actions/admin-client.ts`); every admin page is a thin wrapper. Mutation-tested: a planted tenant import of an admin module, a planted audit delete and a planted barrel re-export each fail it.
- ~~**N4.7** Capture the 10 customers' plan records; record provider-pair counts.~~ **Removed by the owner (Rev 15, 2026-10-12); not pending and not a milestone.** Nothing replaces it. The tenant-page plan fields and the tenants list (N4.3, N4.4) are unchanged.

### Phase N5 — Customer onboarding + retention

**Status:** ✅ 8 of 8 done: 7 merged to `main` (PR #37, 2026-10-02); **N5.8 closed 2026-10-12 (Rev 16) on the owner's confirmation** that the live Zendesk, Intercom and Linear walkthroughs were already completed · **Estimate:** 3–4 weeks · **Priority:** P1 · **Design record:** [N5 notes](#n5-notes) · **Needs:** N2, N4; D26 for N5.3 · **Branch:** `phase/n5-customer-onboarding-and-retention`
**Goal:** any supported pair reaches findings unaided, and existing customers get a recurring reason to return.
**Phase is done when:**

- all four pairs onboard in local dev, and Zendesk + Jira live; _(closed with N5.8 on the owner's confirmation of the live walkthroughs; the per-pair results were not recorded, see N5.8)_;
- a ticket-source-only org gets partial value;
- the monthly report is delivered exactly once per org per month;
- the usage metrics are readable.

- [x] **N5.1** Capability-driven onboarding steps. **Carry-over from N2.7/N2.9:** the wizard's provider-specific flow (two ticket sources, two trackers, Zendesk-only policy review, per-provider connect cards) is rebuilt from `ProviderAdapter.capabilities`, and the `modules/onboarding/`, `lib/onboarding-*.ts`, `lib/types/onboarding.ts` and `actions/onboarding.ts` entries are deleted from `WEB_PROVIDER_NAME_ALLOWED` in `packages/core/test/provider-boundary.test.ts`. **Done 2026-10-02.** The wizard is built from `ProviderAdapter.capabilities`; the onboarding entries are gone from the provider-boundary allowlist. Tests: `onboarding-flow`, `onboarding-progress`, `onboarding-status-matrix`, `onboarding-intercom`. The browser walkthrough is part of N5.8.
- [x] **N5.2** Partial value before a tracker is connected. **Done 2026-10-02.** Findings and dashboard work with a ticket source alone, with a neutral "engineering time appears once a tracker is connected" state and no zero shown as fact. Tests: `partial-value`, `partial-value-ui`.
- [x] **N5.3** Request-access flow for the tracker admin (D26 decided: option b). **Done 2026-10-02.** An owner mints a link from the onboarding tracker step (`POST /api/integrations/connect-links`); only a SHA-256 of the token is stored (`IntegrationConnectLink`), expiry 72 h, scoped to one organization and one provider (Jira or Linear). `/connect/<token>` is public and starts that provider's OAuth with a signed state carrying `connectLinkId`; the Jira and Linear callbacks accept it without a session, consume the link atomically (`updateMany` on `consumedAt: null`, same organization and provider, unexpired) after the grant is known good, and record `Integration.connectedBy`. Not written to `AdminAuditLog`. Tests: `connect-links.test.ts` (real DB: hash-only storage, single use under concurrency, wrong org/provider, expiry, revoke), `connect-link-callback.test.ts` (no session, consumed, provider and org mismatch, forged state).
- [x] **N5.4** Forwardable security summary (needs H-5). **Done 2026-10-02.** `/docs/security`, generated from `getProviderAccessFacts()` (provider registry `access`, i.e. the OAuth scope constants) and `SLACK_BOT_SCOPES`; retention text follows `docs/data-retention-and-on-call.md`. `security-summary.test.ts` pins scopes to the constants and rejects write-capable scope names.
- [x] **N5.5** Link coverage panel. **Done 2026-10-02.** Dashboard link-coverage panel from the shared `link-coverage` function (`certain` links only). Test: `link-coverage-panel`.

Tests: `monthly-report-delivery` (real DB: once per month under concurrent ticks, kill switch, org-local month, tenant scoping) and `monthly-report-render` (content and neutral-language scan).
- [x] **N5.7** Usage instrumentation: weekly active orgs, alert click-through, time to first value. **Done 2026-10-02.** `User.lastSeenAt` (hourly), `Notification.openedAt` (scoped to the session org and the case), `Organization.firstFindingsViewedAt`; best-effort writes. Tests: `usage-tracking`, tenant-isolation.

They were not written up at the time and the repository holds no per-run record: the dates, the provider pairs walked, the minutes from sign-up to first monitored case, hesitations, errors, imported counts and account details are **not recorded**, and none are inferred here. Nothing was run, repeated or requested from the owner for this closure. Independently recorded and unchanged: the local onboarding tests and the stubbed browser walk-through (N1.16) pass; they are not the live evidence. Closes H-9 and the historical 6.8. ### Phase N6 — Entitlements + billing

**Status:** 🟡 9 of 10 done on `main`: entitlements and trial lifecycle (2026-10-02) and the internal billing domain, UI and pricing flow (PR #39, 2026-10-03). **N6.5 (payment provider) is not started:** D28 is closed (2026-10-05), Paymob is the sandbox/testing provider only and the production provider is not chosen. Started before its D20 trigger fired, at the owner's request · **Estimate:** 2–3 weeks once triggered · **Priority:** P2 · **Design record:** [N6 entitlements and billing](#n6-entitlements-and-billing) · **Needs:** N4, N5, D14, D25, D27, D28 · **Branch:** none for N6.1–N6.4 (landed on `main` with the N5 merge, `94254cb`); `feature/billing` (PR #39) for the billing steps
**Trigger:** ≥15 paying tenants, **or** manual plan/invoice work costs more than ~2 h per month, **or** a customer asks for self-serve plan changes. The trigger counts only once D14 is resolved.
**Goal:** the current, intentional pricing is represented once in code; limits are soft; monitoring is never gated by plan state.

- [x] **N6.1** Record D14 and a single plan constant shared by the pricing page and enforcement. **Done 2026-10-02, not deployed.** `packages/db/src/plans.ts` (`PLANS`); `PricingView` and the admin plan labels render from it; table in plan §7. `Organization.plan` stays a string (not tightened to an enum: nothing needed it).
- [x] **N6.2** Usage read model (D25: imported policies do not count). **Done 2026-10-02, not deployed.** `getOrganizationUsage` in `packages/db/src/usage.ts`; shown on the admin tenant page ("Usage vs plan"). No period argument and no `UsageSnapshot` table: the seat model needs neither.
- [x] **N6.3** Soft enforcement at invite, integration connect and native policy creation. **Done 2026-10-02, not deployed.** Over a limit it warns (response field, in-app banner, `EntitlementEvent` on the admin tenant page); it never blocks on a limit. Behind `WorkerSettings.entitlementsEnforced`, **off by default**, set in SQL. A test shows a tenant over every limit still gets evaluated and alerted.
- [x] **N6.4** Trial lifecycle (D27). **Done 2026-10-02, not deployed.** Sign-up now sets `trialEndsAt` to +14 days (it was never set before). When enforced and the trial has ended: new members, integrations and native policies are blocked (402); monitoring, alerts and history are untouched; in-app banner; owner email from the worker's reconciliation tick, once per org and trial end date; event on the admin tenant page. Existing organizations have no `trialEndsAt` and so never lapse until an operator sets one. **UX follow-up 2026-10-02:** the expired-trial banner states that cases, SLA monitoring, alerts and history keep working and offers a contact CTA (`lib/upgrade-cta.ts`, `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPPORT_EMAIL`; no link when unset) instead of `/pricing` and `/sign-up`. A blocked connect lands on `/settings/integrations?entitlement=trial_expired&action=connect&provider=…` with a notice naming what was blocked. The invite form and native-policy dialog show the soft-limit warning when a creation reaches or passes the plan limit (it never blocks). When N6.5 ships, change only `getUpgradeCta()` to return `/upgrade`.
  - ⚠️ **D27 interpretation recorded (2026-10-05), implementation gap open:** the owner interprets D27's "new cases" as newly ingested cases from connected providers. This is **not implemented**: only new configuration (members, integrations, native policies) is blocked, and provider-ingested cases keep being created and evaluated. See D27 for the exact gap. Behavior is deliberately unchanged by the 2026-10-05 documentation cleanup.
- [ ] **N6.5** Payment-provider integration. **Not started; needs an explicit go-ahead.** D28 is closed (2026-10-05): Paymob is the initial **sandbox/testing** provider only, and the **production provider is still open** and must be chosen separately before any production billing go-live. The provider-neutral boundary is built (N6.8). No Paymob code, SDK, credentials, adapter, webhook or UI exists. When it ships, change only `getUpgradeCta()` to return `/upgrade`.
- [x] **N6.7** Provider-neutral internal billing domain. **Done 2026-10-03 (commit `bee4983`, PR #39).** Additive migration `20261004100000_n6_internal_billing` adds `billing_accounts`, `billing_subscriptions` (with a `version` token), `billing_invoices` and `billing_events`; subscription status reuses `PlanStatus`. `packages/db/src/billing.ts`: `start` (trial-aware: during a trial the plan is chosen now and billing starts at `trialEndsAt` with no invoice; otherwise active now with a one-month period and an open net-14 invoice), `change_plan` (upgrades apply now with proration through `prorateUpgradeCents`; downgrades are scheduled for period end as `pendingPlan` and can be withdrawn; changes during a trial apply now with no proration), `change_seats` (within `[max(1, seatsInUse), plan limit]`), `cancel` / `resume` / restart, period renewal and trial conversion on the worker's reconcile tick, `past_due` derived from invoices, and operator overrides (grace, mark paid, void, comp, notes). Every transition runs in one transaction under an organization row lock with optimistic versioning, records a `BillingEvent`, and mirrors plan and status onto the `Organization` columns that the entitlement checks read, so entitlements change atomically with the plan. Licensed seats narrow the plan's seat limit. Typed errors: `invalid_plan`, `invalid_seats`, `contact_sales` (422); `invalid_transition`, `conflict` (409); `provider_unavailable` (501). Pricing stays flat, monthly and in USD (D14); seats never change the price. Tests: `packages/db` unit and real-Postgres suites (`billing.test.ts`, `billing.db.test.ts`) and web suites (`billing.test.ts`, `billing-routes.test.ts`, `admin-billing.test.ts`); tenant-isolation and scope classification cover the four new tables. Reference: [N6 entitlements and billing](#n6-entitlements-and-billing) (N6.7–N6.10).
- [x] **N6.8** Payment-provider boundary (no provider integrated). **Done 2026-10-03 (PR #39).** `packages/db/src/billing-provider.ts` defines the `BillingProvider` interface (subscription sync, payment-method and portal sessions, collecting an invoice); `apps/web/src/lib/billing-provider.ts` `getBillingProvider()` returns `null`, so provider-only operations report `provider_unavailable` and invoices are "Direct invoice" on net-14 terms that an operator marks paid. Integrating a provider means writing an adapter and returning it there (that is N6.5); nothing else in the app names a provider (D28).
- [x] **N6.9** Billing UI and API. **Done 2026-10-03 (PR #39).** Customer `/billing` (current plan, entitlements, invoices; owner-only changes) with `GET/POST /api/billing/{subscription,account,provider-session}`; operator `/admin/billing` and `/admin/billing/[organizationId]` with `/api/admin/billing[/organizationId]`, where each override is audited as `billing_override` in the same transaction as the change. Members read; only owners change; an `internal` organization cannot start a subscription. The provider-session route returns 501 until N6.5.
- [x] **N6.10** Viewer-aware pricing and Review & Subscribe. **Done 2026-10-03 (commit `fbdbbc3`, PR #39).** `/pricing` reads the billing read model for signed-in viewers: visitors go to sign-up, members see a read-only state, owners go to `/billing/subscribe?plan=` (start, switch during trial, upgrade, downgrade, keep, resume), with seat blockers, scheduled changes, past-due and internal organizations reflected from `planOptions`. The review screen resolves one of `start` / `change_plan` / `resume` on the server and shows when it takes effect, what is invoiced and what changes against the plan limits; the upgrade preview shares `prorateUpgradeCents` with the charge. No payment-provider UI. Test: `billing-pricing-flow.test.ts`.
  - **Known gaps in the internal billing (not tracked as tasks; recorded in the N6 design record):** nothing emails an invoice or says how to pay it, there is no PDF, and `paymentMethod` is always `null`. These are for N6.5 or a manual-invoicing follow-up; the UI must not promise an email.
- [x] **N6.6** Public claims match the code. **Done 2026-10-02.** The change-plan FAQ was later rewritten (PR #39, 2026-10-03): it states that only an organization owner can subscribe, change plans or cancel, matching the in-app flow. It no longer says "talk to us". The plan bullets are generated from `PLANS`; the trial FAQ now states what happens at expiry; a test fails if the page names SSO/SAML, retention, proration or "case history".

> **Rev 7 review (2026-10-05).** N6.1–N6.4 and N6.6–N6.10 were re-checked against their tests, which pass in the full suite, and none was reopened: `packages/db/test/plans.test.ts` (N6.1), `entitlements.test.ts` (N6.2, N6.3, N6.4), `apps/web/test/entitlements-gate.test.ts` and `entitlement-ui.test.ts` (N6.3, N6.4), `packages/notifications/test/trial-expiry.test.ts` (N6.4 owner email), `apps/web/test/pricing-plans.test.ts` (N6.6), `packages/db/test/billing.test.ts` and `billing.db.test.ts`, `apps/web/test/billing-routes.test.ts` and `admin-billing.test.ts` (N6.7–N6.9) and `billing-pricing-flow.test.ts` (N6.10). N6.5 stays not started and gated; the D27 gap noted under N6.4 is unchanged.

### Phase N8 — Scale

**Status:** ⏸ Trigger-based per item (D21) · **Priority:** P3 · **Design record:** [N8 scale items](#n8-scale-items-and-process) · **Needs:** N3 metrics · **Branch:** `phase/n8-<item>` per triggered item
**Goal:** fix the limit that is measured to bite next, in the order it bites.

Not decided here._ _Context: scheduling is already per organization with leases and start-to-start due times (Appendix D invariant 11); this item is only a time budget on top._
- [ ] **N8-S2** Resumable, rate-aware backfill (trigger: backfill > 4 h or repeated 429s).

- [ ] **N8-S4** Per-chunk org lock (trigger: webhook latency p95 > 30 s).

### Phase N9 — Custom Ticket Provider

**Status:** 🚧 N9.1–N9.15 implemented and merged to `main` (PR #48 `553592e`; phase branch tip `8cb91d7` is an ancestor of `main`, 2026-10-10). Decisions Q1–Q16, R1–R6, U1, U3–U6 approved; U2 decided for the pilot (option (b)). **N9.14-F1 was removed by the owner (2026-10-12, Rev 17)** · **Estimate:** set after the N9.1 spike · **Priority:** P2 (proposed) · **Design record:** [N9 design record](#n9-design-record) · **Needs:** N2, N3, D31 · **Branch:** `phase/n9-custom-ticket-provider` (small, reviewable PRs permitted; see Branch rules and the N9 exception)
**Goal:** a customer whose helpdesk has no adapter can connect it through a guided UI with no code and no new adapter, as one statically registered, configuration-driven `custom` ticket source (D31), without weakening SLA correctness, tenant isolation or the read-only promise.
**Verification policy:** focused checks only (`pnpm type-check`, build, and the specific tests a change touches when the owner asks for testing). The full suite is not run per PR. CI targets `testing`, not PRs into `main` (see Branch rules); **N9 does not change CI triggers**, and any pre-merge verification policy is a separate, deferred decision. _Rev 10 note (2026-10-10): the "not run / no database / not applied" remarks in the task notes below were true when written (2026-10-09) and are kept as history. N9 is merged (PR #48), and later runs are recorded in `docs/validation/server-validation-master.md` (B-03 to B-16, C-05 to C-08, D-01, D-08), which are owner-recorded results not re-run in this revision._ Shared changes (N9.3, N9.8a, N9.9, N9.10, N9.13) carry the D24 replay requirement and focused regression coverage.

A budget- or cap-limited run ends `partial` (not successful, not a failure); progress is kept through the last fully completed page, with no duplicate events or commitments on resumption; a real failure is never reported as partial; three zero-progress runs become `failed` (`no_progress`) (Q12, R3, R6, N9 design record §6.12). Activation is refused for a source with no incremental cursor that cannot finish its full pass in one run (R6, N9 design record §4.3).

### Phase N10 — Integration Control Center

**Status:** 🟡 N10.1–N10.6 done; merged to `main` through PR #49 (`0e48d28`) and PR #50 (`4316f87`, N10.7 tests); N10.7 closed and the N10-F1 migration written on `phase/n10-f1-contract` (2026-10-11; not applied to production) · **Estimate:** ~1 week · **Priority:** P1 · **Design record:** [N10 design record](#n10-design-record) · **Needs:** N4, N9.8, D33 · **Branch:** `phase/n10-integration-control-center` (parallel-track exception, see Branch rules)
**Goal:** a platform operator controls each provider's availability (enabled, release stage, Beta allowlist) from `/admin/integrations`, without a deployment, and the backend refuses every provider operation that the policy does not allow, without deleting or altering any customer data.
**Verification policy:** `pnpm type-check` and the web build on the phase branch; the focused N10 tests (the suites named in the N10 design record §10) on `testing`, synced only as far as they need (D33 ruling 8). No full-suite run unless the owner asks.
**Phase is done when:**

- every entry point in N10 design record §5.2–§5.3 calls the resolver, and the boundary test proves it;
- disabling and re-enabling a provider is verified for one existing provider and for Custom REST, with no data changed;
- the admin console shows and edits every provider, with audit rows and stale-write protection;
- customers see Unavailable / Coming soon / Paused by Elapsed, never a false "Disconnected".
