# Design Records (migrated from the removed implementation plans, Rev 14)

> **Rev 14 (2026-10-11).** The ten per-phase implementation plans (`01`–`10`) and the two N9 legal-review documents were consolidated into this file and deleted. Only what is still needed to continue the work, or to keep a decision traceable, is carried here. Implemented behavior is documented by the code and its tests; where this file and the code disagree, report it before changing either (Documentation lifecycle, rule 6). The removed files are in git history, last present at commit `d0fdc78`; no copy is kept in the tree.
>
> **Citation rule.** Source comments, docs and the validation ledger still cite "plan NN §x" (for example "plan 09 §6.4", "plan 10 §7.3", "plan 01 §5"). Read each as the same-numbered section here: plan 09 → [N9 design record](#n9-design-record), plan 10 → [N10 design record](#n10-design-record), plan 01 §5 → [Replay/diff gate](#replaydiff-gate-d24), plan 06 §7 → [N6 entitlement table](#n6-entitlements-and-billing), plans 03, 04, 07 and 08 → their subsections below. The section numbers of the N9 and N10 records were kept for that reason.

## Replay/diff gate (D24)

Existing customer behavior must not silently change (D24). Any change to `@sla/core`, `@sla/commitments`, a normalizer, correlation, or customer/case identity merges needs a recorded replay result before it merges.

```text
stored events (restored backup, read-only) → re-evaluate with CURRENT code → baseline capture
apply the change → re-evaluate the SAME stored events at the SAME asOf with NEW code → diff
target: zero unintended status / breachedAt differences
```

- **Compared per commitment:** `status`, `breachedAt` (the `effectiveDueAt` when breached), `effectiveDueAt`, `elapsedSeconds`, `remainingSeconds`, `clock.state`, `clock.pauseCause`, `warnThresholdCrossed`, `inputs.policyVersionId`, `inputs.calendarVersionId` and the evaluation `id`. **Per case:** the policy version `matchPolicyVersion` selects per commitment kind, the Next Reply cycle plan (`deriveNextReplyCycles` keys), `deriveLegSpans` output, and the count of `certain` `CaseLink`s (for correlation changes).
- **L1 (evaluation replay):** stored `NormalizedEvent`, `Commitment`, policy and calendar rows through the pure engine; required for every change to `@sla/core` or `@sla/commitments`. **L2 (normalization replay):** re-run normalization and correlation from `RawEvent` on the scratch database, then L1; required for adapters, correlation, `Customer` or `Case` keys.
- **Where:** only on a scratch database restored from a backup (`scripts/restore-drill.sh` creates `sla_restore_drill`). L1 opens a `READ ONLY` transaction; L2 writes only the scratch database. Never against the live database. Use the same `--as-of` for baseline and after.
- **Classes:** **A, unintended:** fix the change; it cannot merge. **B, intentional:** record decision ID, commitment and tenant counts, before → after, customer-facing effect and owner approval date in the phase entry, with owner approval before merging; finalized commitments may be corrected but raise no new alert (`canRaiseAlert`). **C, pre-existing drift** (the baseline code already disagrees with the persisted status or `breachedAt`): report the count and open a correctness task; it does not block.
- **Customer data rule:** capture files stay in the scratch directory, never in git; only aggregate counts and classifications are recorded.
- **Commands:** `pnpm --filter @sla/commitments replay:capture -- --as-of <ISO> --out <file.jsonl>` and `replay:compare -- <baseline.jsonl> <after.jsonl> --approved <approved.json>` (non-zero exit on any unlisted difference), with `DATABASE_URL` pointing at the scratch database. The host procedure and pass criteria are in [`docs/n2-replay-runbook.md`](../docs/n2-replay-runbook.md).
- **Approved class-B differences to date: none.** **Class C drift log:** N1.2 (2026-09-29 backup, `asOf` 2026-09-29T21:36:44Z): 3,921 commitments, 0 status, 10 `breachedAt` (H-13, closed 2026-10-10). N2.11 (2026-10-01 backup): 3,923 commitments, 0 status, 9 `breachedAt`.
- **Incident rule:** if production evaluations diverge after a deploy, restore customer trust first: revert, run the comparison on a restored backup to find the class, then fix forward. N1's schema changes were expand-only, so code rollback never needed a database rollback; `NormalizedEvent.sourceRole` NOT NULL (`20260930120000_…`) was the one exception, shipped last.

## N2 contract architecture (as built) and the N2.10 release

- **Authority:** `packages/ingestion/src/contract.ts` (`ProviderAdapter`, `CanonicalBatch` with `eventGroups`, `CorrelationOutput`, `recognizeCaseUrl(url, credentials)`, `ProviderWebAdapter`, and the shared errors `ReauthRequiredError`, `PermissionDeniedError`, `ProviderUnavailableError`, `IntegrationNotConfiguredError`). Read that file, not a copy of it.
- **Rules (D16, amended by D31):** registries are static literals with `satisfies Record<IntegrationProvider, …>` (`apps/worker/src/providers.ts`, `apps/web/src/lib/providers.ts`; no shared registry package), so a missing provider is a compile error. `@sla/ingestion` depends on `@sla/core`, `@sla/db` and `@sla/logger`; provider packages import only types and error classes from it, never the projector, and never `@sla/commitments`. The projector lives in `@sla/ingestion` because it cannot live in a provider package and must not live in `@sla/commitments`. Only what current code needs goes in the contract. The Conversation is rendered only through the source adapter's `renderConversation`; the engine never reads raw events; persisting normalized message bodies is out of scope.
- **N2.10 release procedure** (the migration is held out of the tree on purpose): files are in `packages/db/prisma/contract/20261001110000_contract_customer_identity_and_case_source/` (migration, `rollback.sql`, `schema.patch`, README). To ship: (1) fix the ledger C-09 prerequisite (`apps/web/test/integration-disconnect-visibility.test.ts:333` does not type-check on the contracted schema); (2) take a fresh verified backup per `docs/production-backup-runbook.md`; (3) move the directory into `prisma/migrations`, apply `schema.patch`, deploy it alone, with the previous release's image available; (4) re-run the L2 replay of `docs/n2-replay-runbook.md`; (5) then tick N2.10 and N2.11. It is the only non-expand-only N2 step; rolling back after it needs `rollback.sql` (re-adds the three `Customer` columns and repopulates them from `CustomerIdentity`; checksum-verified on a restore on 2026-10-05).

## N3 freshness rules and the N3.7 circuit-breaker design

- **Freshness is metadata, never time math.** `assessFreshness` (`packages/core/src/freshness.ts`) is pure and provider-free; the default grace is 3 × the active-poll interval (`WorkerSettings.freshnessGraceFactor`, operator-only). It never changes `elapsedSeconds`, `status` or `breachedAt`. `Evaluation.sourceStaleSince` is the earliest `staleSince` of the case's source integration and of any tracker integration with a `certain` link on the case; it is written on the evaluation rows already being written, and a freshness change alone never creates an `Evaluation` (`shouldPersistEvaluation`). Evaluation always runs; monitoring is never hard-stopped for a tenant.
- **D13(b) mechanics:** a held breach alert must not consume its `(commitmentId, threshold)` claim; `claimNotifications` skips held candidates, which become eligible on the first fresh tick; `canRaiseAlert`'s finalized-commitment rule still applies. The provider name in the staleness caveat comes from the integration row, not a code branch.
- **N3.7, only if triggered (D22 gate unchanged):** after `consecutiveFailures ≥ 3` with `ProviderUnavailableError`, skip that integration's **ingest** until `Integration.nextAttemptAt` (additive, nullable); exponential backoff capped at the reconciliation interval; normalization and evaluation still run; reauth and permission-denied states are not outages and are unchanged; an operator switch in `WorkerSettings` disables it without a deploy. **Trigger:** production data or a real outage shows one failing integration adding more than 25% of the active-poll interval to a **healthy** organization's run (read as defined in N3.6). If the trigger is not met, record "not needed, measured at X ms" in N3.7. Optionally add an ops alert for an integration failing for more than a configurable number of hours.
- **Update note (Rev 8, kept):** `@sla/http-retry` sets a per-attempt timeout (30 s via `AbortSignal.timeout`), up to 5 attempts and up to 60 s of total waiting, so the worst case for one request is about 210 s; any budget that wraps provider calls must be sized against that figure.

## N4 authorization boundaries

| Actor | How resolved | Can see | Can never see |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Platform admin (operator) | `PLATFORM_ADMIN_EMAILS` via `isPlatformOperator` (kept at this scale) | All tenants' metadata, integration health, plan records, failures, worker health | Credentials, OAuth secrets, `IntegrationConfig.clientSecret` (never decrypted for display) |
| Org owner | `UserRole.owner` | Own org's integration health, freshness, link coverage, plan and usage (read-only) | Other tenants; `WorkerSettings`; admin notes; audit log; plan overrides |
| Org member | `UserRole.member` | Own org's operational data | The same, plus owner-only settings |

Admin pages are `notFound()` and admin APIs and actions 403 for everyone who is not an operator; admin data modules are never imported from tenant code (`admin-boundary.test.ts`). **Never exposed to customers:** `WorkerSettings`, circuit-breaker controls, cross-tenant data, admin notes, the audit log, plan overrides, raw provider error internals, credentials. **Later (≈500 tenants):** database-backed admin roles, consent-based impersonation, general feature flags (only provider availability was pulled forward, D33), SLO dashboards, per-tenant worker sharding. **P1 (≈50 tenants):** tenant data export and deletion workflow (after the H-5 retention answer), support notes.

## N5 notes

N5.8 requires live walkthroughs with real sandboxes: **Zendesk + Jira required**; Intercom + Jira when a sandbox exists; Zendesk + Linear optional; record time-to-findings per pair with dates here and under H-9 / 6.8. **Closed (Rev 16, 2026-10-12):** on the owner's confirmation that the walkthroughs were completed; the per-pair record asked for here was not kept and the closure was made without it (see N5.8). The monthly report has an operator kill switch (`WorkerSettings.monthlyReportEnabled`); PDF reports, portals, AI summaries, financial figures and third-party analytics are out of scope.

## N6 entitlements and billing

**Entitlement table** (D14; implemented once in `packages/db/src/plans.ts` `PLANS`, which the pricing page renders; `null` is unlimited):

| Plan id | Price | Seats | Support integrations | Engineering integrations | Native policies |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `starter` | $49 / month | 5 | 1 | 1 | 3 |
| `team` | $149 / month | 20 | unlimited | unlimited | unlimited |
| `enterprise` | Custom | unlimited | unlimited | unlimited | unlimited |

Definitions (`packages/db/src/usage.ts`): **seats** = members + pending, unexpired invitations; **support integration** = a connected `ticket_source`; **engineering integration** = a connected `work_tracker` or `code_host` (only `disconnected` rows are not counted); **native policies** = native, non-archived (imported never count, D25).

Behavior (`packages/db/src/entitlements.ts`), only at invite, connect and native-policy create, and only while `WorkerSettings.entitlementsEnforced` is on (off by default; set with `UPDATE worker_settings SET "entitlementsEnforced" = true;`, no UI): `internal`, no recorded plan, or a `null` limit → allow; trial running → allow; under the limit → allow; at or over the limit → **warn** (the creation goes ahead, the response carries `entitlementWarning`, an `EntitlementEvent` is recorded, an in-app banner appears; D14 requires no hard block); trial ended (D27) → **blocked** (HTTP 402; connect routes redirect to settings) for new members, integrations and native policies, while cases, SLA monitoring, alerts, history and existing data are untouched. The D27 "new cases" gap is recorded under D27. Customer wording says monitoring continues and uses one replaceable call to action, `apps/web/src/lib/upgrade-cta.ts` (contact link from `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPPORT_EMAIL`, plain text if unset); N6.5 changes only that to `/upgrade`.

**N6.5 specification (not started; needs an explicit go-ahead).** An adapter behind `getBillingProvider()` (`apps/web/src/lib/billing-provider.ts`) implementing the `BillingProvider` interface (`packages/db/src/billing-provider.ts`); it must not change the subscription/plan domain. Checkout, the customer portal, and a signed webhook (`apps/web/src/app/api/billing/webhook/route.ts`, idempotent by event id, signature-verified) that feeds the internal billing domain; provider customer id stored on `Organization` (`billingCustomerId`, only if the provider needs it); manual overrides stay possible and audited (`billing_override`). D28: Paymob is the sandbox/testing provider only; the production provider is chosen separately, before any production go-live, on Egypt support (cards, wallets such as Vodafone Cash, Fawry/reference-code), international availability, recurring billing, currencies, fees, settlement, webhook reliability and business/legal requirements. Tests: signed-fixture webhook tests and a replay-safe idempotency test. A `UsageSnapshot` table is needed only if a volume metric is ever adopted (not under D14).

**Billing UI constraints that hold until N6.5 ships.** The billing UI must not show: a card or bank form, provider branding, hosted checkout, "Pay now", payment-method management, or a "Manage in billing portal" action (the existing disabled button stays); fake payment confirmations or receipts, an invoice PDF, any claim that an invoice is emailed, or specific bank or payment instructions; an annual/monthly toggle, discount or promo codes, tax or VAT, multi-currency, seat purchasing or per-seat price math (pricing is flat), usage-based billing or overages, a free tier, "pause subscription", self-serve trial extension, or customer-facing dunning; terms or consent checkboxes; features that do not exist (SSO, custom retention). Vocabulary stays provider-agnostic ("invoice", "billing email", "direct invoice"); the review shows one "Payment" row reading "Direct invoice, net 14"; the confirm action must tolerate a slower or failing confirm (a provider failure rolls the change back). **Read-model gaps recorded 2026-10-03, not re-verified:** a first-invoice preview for an unchosen plan (`cycleLines`); `start` accepts an optional `seatQuantity` that no UI sends; `SignInForm` ignores `callbackUrl`. Out of scope for N6: metered billing beyond D14, custom invoicing, tax logic beyond a provider's defaults, seat-level SSO, anything that pauses monitoring. Rollback: enforcement is behind `entitlementsEnforced`; `getBillingProvider()` returning `null` restores internal-only mode.

## N7 spike and acceptance (Zoho Desk, D29)

- **N7.1 spike (≤ 1 week, sandbox account):** capture real payloads (tickets, status history or audits, replies, organizations, priorities, and the URL format of links created by its Jira and Linear integrations) as fixtures in the new package's `test/fixtures/`; map every status to an existing `NormalizedState` (if one cannot be mapped without a new semantic state, **stop**: that is a roadmap decision, not an adapter change); fill the mapping table below. Checks: a read-only OAuth or API-token model; an incremental change API or audit log; an account/company concept for `CustomerIdentity` (or a documented fallback like Intercom's contact); tickets linked to Jira or Linear in a way the tracker exposes as a URL or reference. The technical kill criteria are `d29-zoho-desk-due-diligence.md` §13; if one fails, N7 stops and the failure is recorded here.
- **Shape:** `packages/<provider>` mirrors `packages/intercom` (`client`, `oauth`, `tokenLifecycle`, `backfill`, `rawEvents`, `normalize`, `hash`, `types`, `index`, `test/`), depending on `@sla/core` (types), `@sla/db`, `@sla/http-retry` and `@sla/ingestion` (types and errors only). Adapter record: `role: "ticket_source"`, honest `capabilities` (`webhooks: false` unless a receiver is built), `ingest` with `http-retry` and the N3 timeouts, `normalize` returning a `CanonicalBatch` (priorities mapped to `CanonicalPriority`), `recognizeCaseUrl` for the org's own tenant host only (reject lookalikes and other tenants), web adapter `externalUrl` and `renderConversation`. The registration surface is the checklist in [N9 design record §9.2](#n9-design-record); the Rev 8 correction there supersedes the shorter list formerly in plan 07.
- **Acceptance:** zero lines changed in `packages/core`, `packages/commitments`, `packages/notifications` and `packages/ingestion/src/projector.ts` (`git diff --stat main -- …` is empty); six matrix pairs pass (`provider-matrix-smoke.test.ts` extended with the new provider + Jira and + Linear); L1 + L2 replay for existing tenants shows 0 differences; labelled Beta (D17). If a core change turns out to be necessary, document it first as a **contract gap** (fixed in `@sla/ingestion` with replay) or a **domain gap** (roadmap decision); N7 is not done by quietly editing the core. Out of scope: webhooks (unless trivially available and verified live), policy import (native policies cover it, like Intercom, D9), promotion out of Beta, a second new provider in the same phase. Rollback: the provider is additive; the `IntegrationProvider` enum value is never removed.

## N8 scale items and process

Principles: snapshots for reading, live evaluation for looking; bound the work per tick, then parallelise; never hard-stop monitoring; measure before indexing (`EXPLAIN ANALYZE`, `PERF_METRICS=1`); the engine stays pure; scale the business before the system; every change to evaluation scope passes L1 replay with 0 class-A differences. Measurement tooling: `pnpm --filter @sla/web perf:baseline`, `pnpm --filter @sla/worker perf:baseline` (scope with `PERF_ORG_ID`), `pnpm db:seed:perf-baseline`, `PERF_METRICS=1`, lock logging in `packages/db/src/organization-lock.ts`, `organization_finished.durationMs` and `Integration.lastSyncDurationMs` (N3.6), and `plans/performance-plan.md`.

| # | Limit | Trigger | Work | Verify |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| S1 | Organizations are processed up to a fixed concurrency per worker (workers can be added; adding workers is the first lever) | Active-poll tick p95 > 50% of the interval for 7 days, or one tenant > 25% of the tick (needs rebasing on `organization_finished.durationMs`; not decided) | Per-org time budget and round-robin fairness in `apps/worker/src/cycle.ts` | Worker perf baseline with a multi-org seed; p95 back under 50% |
| S2 | Provider rate limits on large backfills | A backfill > 4 h, or repeated 429s | Resumable, rate-aware backfill with live counts (also serves N5) | Backfill resumes after a worker restart without re-fetching |
| S3 | Evaluation loads every event of every active commitment | Org sweep > 30 s, or lock hold > 10 s | Evaluate only commitments whose events, policy or calendar changed since the last tick | **L1 replay 0 differences** at several `asOf` points; perf baseline |
| S4 | Org-level SLA lock held for the whole sweep | Webhook latency p95 > 30 s for large tenants | Per-chunk lock in the sweep | `organization-lock.test.ts`; `scoped-pipelines.test.ts` |
| S5 | Single web instance (per-instance rate limiting) | Sustained traffic, or an availability commitment | Shared rate limiting (`lib/rate-limit.ts`, `auth-rate-limit.ts`) in Postgres, then 2+ web instances behind `apps/nginx` (R-7) | Rate-limit tests across two instances |
| S6 | Unbounded `RawEvent` / `NormalizedEvent` / `Evaluation` growth | Backup or restore time > 30 min, or analytics p95 regressions | Retention policy (H-5), time partitioning, archive raw events past the replay horizon | Restore drill timing; replay still possible within the horizon |
| S7 | Encryption keys cannot rotate without downtime | A security questionnaire requiring rotation, or suspected key exposure | Dual-key read during rotation (S-7) in `packages/db/src/crypto.ts` and `scripts/rotate-secrets.sh` | Rotation drill without downtime |
| S8 | Postgres as queue, lock manager and store | Contention visible after S1–S4 | Read replica for analytics first; a real queue is a last resort | Measured contention drop |

Per triggered item: record the trigger evidence (metric, date) in the N8 row; capture a perf baseline (and a replay baseline for S3/S4); implement on `phase/n8-<item>`; re-run and attach the numbers to the PR; update `plans/performance-plan.md`. Out of scope until measured: horizontal scaling beyond what Appendix D invariant 11 already ships, a queue system, sharding, caching layers, indexes without `EXPLAIN ANALYZE` evidence.

## N9 design record

> Migrated from plan 09 (2026-10-09 to 2026-10-11). **The code is authoritative for behavior** (`packages/custom-ticket`, `packages/safe-http`, `apps/web/src/lib/custom-provider`, `apps/worker/src/sync-runs.ts`); this record keeps the rules and their reasons. The owner's decisions are summarized in D31 (Q11–Q16, R1–R6) and listed below; the original tags were [approved] (owner decision of 2026-10-09 unless dated), [verified] (checked in the repository) and [proposed] (not a decision). The full Q1–Q10 text, the configuration-document example and the working tables were in plan 09; the example document shape is `packages/custom-ticket/src/schema.ts`.

**§2.1 Rulings not restated in D31.** **Q1** provider-blind `Integration.slaSupport` (null for every existing provider); unsupported kinds are excluded in the shared commitments pipeline, not hidden in the UI. **Q2** failed-record guard has a minimum count (below). **Q4** 120 s ingest wall-clock budget. **Q5** turning the Beta flag off (§8.7). **Q7** comment visibility acknowledgement (§5.2). **Q8/Q16** secrets (§8.4). **Q9** deferred: the pre-merge verification policy stays a separate decision; N9 changes no CI trigger. **Q10** approved as a separate, copy-only change to the five public `/docs/integrations/*` pages (the "60 minutes" → 30 minutes reconciliation figure), on branch `copy/docs-reconciliation-30min`, which is in `main`. **U1** override authority is the organization **owner only** in V1; no administrator role or delegation is built (adding one needs its own decision). **U3** option (a): block activating a configuration version that re-supports `next_reply` while Q14-cancelled `next_reply` commitments exist. **U4** a pass that completes with record failures below the guard is `ok` and advances `lastSuccessfulSyncAt`; failed tickets are reported, shown and retried every pass. **U5** three consecutive zero-progress runs make the next one `failed` (`no_progress`). **U6** support-assisted override: a distinct allowlist beside `PLATFORM_ADMIN_EMAILS` (`GUARD_OVERRIDE_OPERATOR_EMAILS`), the durable `GuardOverride` record, audit action `apply_guard_override`; customer-side expiry 24 h (proposed, settled by the N9.11 review; ledger OD-10). **OD-08** (2026-10-10): Beta ceiling 1,000 live cases per custom integration, provisional (§6.9–§6.10). **U2** (2026-10-10): option (b) for the pilot only (§6.11). **Reaffirmed:** raw-event pruning stays deferred (H-5); no synthetic status history; no closure timestamp inferred from `updatedAt`; no deletion based on absence from a sync response.

### §4 Capabilities

- **§4.1 Configuration:** a versioned JSON document (`schemaVersion: 1`) validated by a strict Zod schema (unknown keys rejected, sizes and counts bounded); the same validator runs on draft save, activation and worker load. It never contains a secret.
- **§4.2 Endpoints:** `tickets` (required), `comments` (embedded path or per-ticket `{{ticket.id}}`), `ticketDetail` (the only way to establish deletion by verified 404), `statusHistory` (real transitions only). Methods: `GET`, or `POST` with `readOnlySearch: true` set only by the owner's explicit confirmation. Variables (`{{cursor}}`, `{{page}}`, `{{offset}}`, `{{limit}}`, `{{updatedSince}}`, `{{ticket.id}}`) substitute only into URL-encoded path segments, query values and JSON string values, never into scheme, host or port.
- **§4.3 Pagination and incremental sync:** page number, offset/limit, cursor, same-origin next URL, `Link` header (RFC 8288 `rel="next"`, same-origin); each has page and item caps and repeated-cursor detection. Incremental by an updated-since parameter (ISO 8601, epoch seconds or milliseconds) with a configurable look-back. **R6:** activation is blocked for a source with no usable incremental cursor that cannot finish its initial full pass inside one run's limits; the check is empirical (the validator runs the full listing under the same limits and budget) and refuses with the measured pages, tickets, requests and seconds. A source with a cursor may take many runs to import history (resumable) and is shown as an import in progress, not a fault.
- **§4.4 Authentication:** API key in a header, Bearer, Basic, custom header; no OAuth; secrets write-only (§8.4). 401 → `ReauthRequiredError` / `reauth_required`; 403 → `PermissionDeniedError` / `permission_denied`.
- **§4.5 Field mapping:** constrained JSONPath subset (`$`, `.key`, `['key']`, `[n]`, `[*]`; no filters, recursion, expressions or functions; `__proto__`, `constructor`, `prototype` rejected). Fixed transforms: `trim`, `lowercase`, `coalesce`, `template`, `stripHtml`, `epochToIso`, `parseDate(format, timezone)`, `valueMap`, `constant`. Required: external ID (stable and unique within the integration), created at (→ `Case.openedAt`, set once, never updated), current status (explicit value map; drives `closedAt`). Recommended: title, priority, customer ID and name, updated at (watermark only, not stored), ticket URL template. V1 custom fields: tags and channel. A resolved/closed timestamp is required to enable Resolution tracking; comments are required for Full SLA. **No canonical home, deliberately not added:** description, requester id and email, assignee id (D10 stores only a display name), the source's updated-at.
- **§4.6 Status, priority, dates:** every external status maps to one of the eight `NormalizedState` values; an unknown value fails the record (`unknown_status`) unless the owner configures an explicit fallback to `open`. An unmapped priority becomes `null` with a diagnostic (`unknown_priority`), never a pass-through string. Dates: ISO 8601 with an offset, or epoch; a date with no timezone requires an explicitly configured IANA timezone (UTC and the organization timezone are never assumed); a nonexistent or ambiguous local time is rejected (`nonexistent_local_time`, `ambiguous_local_time`).

### §5 SLA modes and event history

- **§5.1 What the engine needs:** `first_response` needs a ticket-source `case_created` with a correct actor (an agent-created ticket starts the clock at the first customer reply, D5b; the commitment is created once and never re-derived) and completion by `agent_replied` else `case_closed` (a reply-less close is never `met`, D5); `resolution` needs `case_closed` and `state_changed` for reopens (pauses on `resolved` always, D3, and for native policies on `pending_customer`, D30); `next_reply` needs `customer_replied` and `agent_replied`. Custom sources use **native** policies only (priority and customer matching).
- **§5.2 Modes:** **Full SLA** (tickets plus reply data with reliable author roles; the creation actor must be derivable: explicit creator-role path, first-comment author role, or an explicit assertion that tickets are customer-created) and **Resolution-only** (first response and next reply are unsupported and shown as such). **U7:** a terminal status without a reliable source-supplied closure timestamp blocks Resolution tracking; `updatedAt` is never substituted. **Q7:** with no reliable public/private field, Full SLA needs the owner's explicit acknowledgement that all returned comments are customer-visible, recorded inside the immutable configuration version (`commentVisibilityAcknowledged`, who, when), never defaulted, and re-confirmed when the comments endpoint or mapping changes; without it the source can still run Resolution-only. The validator shows each metric as `supported`, `supported with limitations` or `unsupported`.
- **§5.3 History is optional and never invented:** real status history is imported at real timestamps; otherwise only the current status and source-supplied timestamps are used; Elapsed never synthesizes `state_changed` or `case_closed` from polling observations. **Current-status-only limitations:** no customer-wait pause (Resolution time can read longer), reopen detection limited to the net current state, sparse timeline, Resolution alerts computed on incomplete data (caveat shown).
- **§5.4 Enforcement:** `Integration.slaSupport` (e.g. `{ "unsupportedKinds": ["first_response","next_reply"], "limitations": ["no_status_history"] }`) is written at activation; `runCommitmentPipeline`, `runNextReplyCyclePipeline` and `runCommitmentReResolutionPipeline` read it (worker, connect-time sync and webhook paths all call these), so one gate covers all three; `null` leaves existing providers unchanged (D24: 0 differences). Notices appear on the integration page and case detail; at-risk and breach alerts for affected Resolution calculations carry a caveat line (`alertCaveatFor`, N9.13; Beta enablement was blocked until N9.13 and N9.8a shipped).
- **§5.5 Existing commitments that become unsupported (Q14):** activating a version (including a rollback) whose `unsupportedKinds` includes a kind the integration already has commitments for requires a dry-run and explicit confirmation. Never delete a commitment, evaluation or notification; cancel only unfinalized (`closedAt` null and status not `cancelled`, `ACTIVE_COMMITMENT_WHERE`) commitments of the newly unsupported kind; finalized history untouched; idempotent. The dry-run groups by kind and status (listing `breached` with `closedAt` null separately), shows what is kept, up to 20 sample case ids, and a hash of the exact commitment id set; confirmation is owner-only, bound to one integration, one target version and that hash (a changed set voids it); cancellation and activation commit together with an audit record (`CustomActivationAudit`). D24 checks: existing providers unaffected; custom differences are only the previewed cancellations; a second run is a no-op; finalized commitments and every evaluation byte-identical; nothing deleted.
- **§5.6 Rollback and cancelled commitments (R5, from the real pipeline):** `runCommitmentPipeline` never recreates or restores a cancelled `first_response` or `resolution` (a cancelled row counts as present, and `@@unique([caseId, kind, cycleKey])` forbids a second); nothing else cancels those kinds; `planCycleCommitments` **silently restores** a cancelled `next_reply` whose cycle is derived again, with its old clock and no reason recorded. Policy: a rollback never reactivates a cancelled commitment; `first_response`/`resolution` stay cancelled; for `next_reply`, activation of a version that re-supports it is blocked while Q14-cancelled commitments exist (U3 option (a)); restoring cancelled commitments needs a new, separately reviewed reconciliation path (not part of N9) that decides whether a restored commitment resumes its old clock (a breach is final, D2) or starts a new one.

### §6 Synchronization, deletion and auditability

- **§6.1 Flow:** initial import last 90 days by default (up to 365), resumable (cursor persisted after every completed page; `cursor.backfillCompletedAt` set only when the whole window is read). Each worker tick runs `ingest` outside the organization lock (raw events and cursor only), then `syncIntegration` normalizes and projects inside it; no per-integration interval in V1. The incremental-poll / full-sweep normalization design (Q13) remains a proposal, so `incrementalNormalization` stays `false` unless §6.10's correctness and performance evidence is produced.
- **§6.2 Limits:** per-attempt timeout 30 s (bounded by the remaining budget); run budget 120 s; 50 pages and 5,000 tickets per run; 5 MB per response and 50 MB per run; 5 requests per second; JSON depth 20; stored payload 64 KB (larger fails its record `payload_too_large`, never truncated); live-case ceiling provisional and configurable (§6.4, Beta value 1,000).
- **§6.3 Budget (Q4, Q12):** one 120 s budget per ingest run on a monotonic clock covering every attempt, back-off, rate-limit wait and child request (not the DB-only stages after). Every request is bounded by the remaining budget (`attemptTimeoutMs = min(30 s, remaining)`; `Retry-After` and back-offs clamped; no attempt begins with under 1 s left). On expiry the in-flight request is aborted, its response discarded, nothing from an incomplete page is written, the cursor stays at the last completed page, and the run ends `partial` with reason `budget_exhausted`. Arithmetic, not a measurement: 5 requests per second over 120 s allows at most 600 requests per run, so per-ticket comment requests bind first (about 500–600 tickets per run).
- **§6.4 Guards (all run inside `normalize()` before a batch is returned, so an abort writes nothing):** definitions at the start of the pass under the organization lock: **L** live cases of the integration (whole integration in both pass modes); **B** distinct tickets in the pass; **F** failing members of B; **D** distinct live external ids in the deletion set; **R** live cases whose derived lifecycle state (open/closed) differs from stored; **N** distinct pass tickets that are not live cases.

  | Guard | Aborts when | Reason code |
  | --- | --- | --- |
  | Mass deletion | `D > max(3, 0.05 × L)` (no rounding) | `mass_deletion` |
  | Failed records (Q2) | `F ≥ 3` and `F > 0.25 × B` | `mass_record_failure` |
  | Mass lifecycle change (Q3, Q11) | `R ≥ 10` and `R > 0.25 × L` | `mass_lifecycle_change` |
  | Live-case ceiling (Q13) | `L + N > C` | `live_case_ceiling` |

  When the failed-record guard does not fire, failed records are excluded and reported individually (`{recordId, code}`), the rest projects, and the failed tickets' stored state is unchanged. An abort records reason, counts and up to 20 record ids on the sync run and `Integration.lastSyncError`, leaves cases, events, evaluations and the watermark untouched, and **repeats on every pass until the cause is resolved**. The ceiling `C` is `CUSTOM_PROVIDER_LIVE_CASE_CEILING` (integer 1 to 100,000, default 1,000), read once per normalization pass per integration by the worker (`liveCaseCeiling()`); a ceiling of 1,000 allows exactly 1,000 live cases and the pass that would make 1,001 aborts with nothing written; the web app never evaluates it. Boundary rows that must stay tested: failed records (B=3,F=3 abort; B=12,F=3 no; B=12,F=4 abort; B=100,F=25 no; B=100,F=26 abort); lifecycle (L=8,R=8 no; L=30,R=9 no; L=39,R=10 abort; L=40,R=10 no; L=40,R=11 abort; L=100,R=25 no; L=100,R=26 abort); deletion with L=101 the threshold is 5.05.
- **§6.5 Deletion:** a ticket is never deleted because it is absent from a list. Authoritative only on a reliable source signal (a status value or boolean path the owner maps as "deleted") or a verified re-fetch (`ticketDetail` 404); either writes a `ticket_deleted:{id}:{hash}` raw event. The projector never clears `deletedAt`; a ticket restored at the source stays hidden in V1 (undelete deferred). A status/flag signal is evaluated on the latest snapshot and clears itself when the ticket is restored; a verified-404 marker is permanent in V1.
- **§6.6 Idempotency:** raw events unique on `(integrationId, providerEventId)` (snapshots add a content hash); cases on `(organizationId, sourceIntegrationId, externalId)`; events reconciled by `diffNormalizedEvents`; re-running a pass creates no duplicate.
- **§6.7 Sync runs (D32):** one `IntegrationSyncRun` per attempt (start/end, trigger, outcome `ok|partial|failed|aborted`, a reason code for every non-`ok` outcome, config version, counts, failure count, up to 50 `{recordId, code}` failures with no payload text), retained 30 days, pruned by the reconciliation tick, recorded for all providers. **Not stored:** a successful run that changed no data and recorded no failure. A data change is decided by before/after comparison in the shared projector (cases created or changed in `customerId`, `subject`, `assigneeName`, `priority`, `channel`, `closedAt` and, when supplied, `requesterName`, `tier`, `tags`, `attributes`; cases soft-deleted; customers created or renamed; events created or deleted; links changed; calendar or policy versions created or policies archived); the comparison only reads. `casesWritten` records cases actually created or changed. Two stored timestamps: *last checked* = `Integration.lastSuccessfulSyncAt` (every successful run), *last data update* = `Integration.lastDataChangedAt` (only runs that changed data, including a partial or failed run whose projection did); both are written in the `Integration` update the worker already makes.
- **§6.8 Configuration versions:** immutable once created; activation sets `Integration.activeConfigVersion` by compare-and-set; rollback re-activates an earlier version. Validation before activation: schema, connectivity, mapping against a live sample, the §5 mode checks. Mapping changes are forward-only by default (`Case.openedAt` and `Commitment.startedAt` do not move); a change that could alter derived events or commitments requires the dry-run diff (`deriveBatch` over stored snapshots compared with `diffNormalizedEvents`, without writing); a configuration needing a field the stored projection lacks needs a re-import and shows its cost.
- **§6.9 Capacity (Q13, R4):** the cost of re-deriving the whole stored set on every pass is not acceptable as a standing design, and 20,000 is not supported by any measurement (`docs/capacity-limits.md` measured to 10,000 and excluded provider normalization and projection; the projector issues about three round trips per case inside the organization lock). Until a ceiling is validated it is provisional, configurable and fails safely; never truncate, rewrite partially or skip the oldest tickets; the benchmark must run with unpruned snapshot growth.
- **§6.10 Benchmark gate and result:** method: synthetic custom-shaped data through the real `deriveBatch`, guards and projector (not rows inserted directly), tiers 1,000 / 5,000 / 10,000 / 20,000 live cases, realistic snapshots and comments per ticket up to the 64 KB cap, full pass, incremental pass at 1%/5%/25% changed, first activation, a guard abort; a fresh scratch database; a second organization and waiting webhooks running concurrently; at least five runs per cell (median and p95). Pass criteria per tier: organization sweep p95 ≤ **30 s** and organization-lock hold p95 ≤ **10 s** (both database-side; neither includes provider HTTP ingestion, which has its own 120 s budget), memory comfortably inside the worker limit, query count and lock time no worse than linear. **Result (2026-10-10, OD-08):** harness `apps/worker/scripts/bench/custom-rest/` on a pinned local 2-CPU host, Postgres 16: 1,000 passes both limits; 1,500 passes and 2,000 fails on a finer grid; 5,000 fails lock hold (p95 about 20.5 s); 10,000 and 20,000 fail both; cost is linear at about 3.9–4.9 ms per case, dominated by the shared projector's per-case writes. Accepted only as a **provisional Beta safeguard (C = 1,000)**, not proof of production-host performance; omitted scenarios are not claimed as passed; raw reports are in `docs/validation/evidence/d-01-benchmark/` and the table is in the ledger (D-01 RESULT). **Follow-ups:** (1) a **real-host benchmark is required before the ceiling is raised** (production host class, CPU/memory/Postgres settings and warm and cold cache recorded, the production worker running concurrently, same two limits at the proposed tier, five runs per cell, second-organization and waiting-webhook cells included; then the owner raises `CUSTOM_PROVIDER_LIVE_CASE_CEILING` to the largest passing tier); (2) **projector write batching** is a separate performance item (shared code for every provider): byte-identical `Case`, `NormalizedEvent`, `CaseLink` and commitment output to the current projector on the D24 replay set and randomized batches including failure isolation, no change to `casesChanged`/`eventsCreated`/`eventsDeleted`, `createdAt` retention or conditional writes, at least a 3× projection-time reduction per case at 1,000 and 5,000 cases and lock-hold p95 at 5,000 cases ≤ 10 s, existing projector, ingestion and provider-matrix suites unchanged. **Correctness criteria before the incremental design can be adopted:** equivalence with a full pass after randomized ingest/incremental sequences; watermark reset replays identically; crash safety (an aborted pass never moves the watermark); out-of-order commits and look-back overlaps not missed; deletion and lifecycle results equal to a full pass, with `L`, `R`, `D`, `N` computed over the whole integration in both modes; forced full pass after configuration change, at activation and on operator request; D24 replay 0 differences for every existing provider.
- **§6.11 Override after a guard abort (Q15, R1, R2, U1, U2, U6):** there is no automatic bypass. Only the **mass lifecycle change** guard can be overridden; an override never bypasses the failed-record guard, the ceiling, the wall-clock budget, request limits, tenant isolation, security validation, the Beta flag or owner-only authorization, and **never forces a mass deletion**. Customer path (owner only; a `member` can never authorize): preview (guard, `R`, `L`, ratio, record ids, the open/closed flips), required reason, a narrowly scoped confirmation bound to one integration, one guard and the preview hash (single use, expiring, 24 h), applied once on the next pass; a changed record set voids it. Support-assisted path: a separate permission (`GUARD_OVERRIDE_OPERATOR_EMAILS`; being a platform operator is not enough), the owner's recorded authorization for the same preview hash (the operator can neither create nor assert it), a durable `GuardOverride` record (path, operator, authorizing owner, reason, preview hash, counts, outcome, `expiresAt`, `consumedAt`; not pruned with sync history) and an `AdminAuditLog` row (`apply_guard_override`). **U2 (decided 2026-10-10, pilot only): option (b):** a deletion abort blocks that integration's syncs and leaves its data visible; no override, no raw-event deletion, no guard bypass; operator procedure in `docs/custom-provider-deletion-recovery.md` (pilots are configured without `deletion.verifyWithDetail`; it cannot apply a legitimate mass deletion or undo a verified-404 marker). **Option (a), a reviewed cleanup workflow, must be reconsidered before general availability.**
- **§6.12 Partial runs and failures (Q12, R3, R6, U4, U5):** a run that ends **only because** its time or record budget is exhausted is `partial`, not successful and not a failure. Classification, in order: (1) 401/403, `credentials_unreadable`, not configured, or an unexpected exception → `failed` with the existing failure policy; (2) a provider or transport error that exhausted retries (5xx, network, TLS, `bad_response`, 429 that did not clear), including when the budget ran out during that retry → `failed` (the budget never turns a failing provider into `partial`); (3) a guard abort or a normalization/projection error → `aborted` (or `failed` for an error); (4) only the budget or a per-run cap → `partial` (writes `lastSyncAt` and duration; leaves `lastSuccessfulSyncAt`, `consecutiveFailures`, `failingSince`, `lastSyncError` untouched); (5) the final page completed and normalization succeeded → `ok`. Reasons for `partial` are `budget_exhausted` or `run_cap_reached`, recorded in the sync run separately from failure codes. Completed pages with record failures: below the guard and complete → `ok` (U4); ended on budget with failures below the guard → `partial`; ended on budget and the failure guard fires → `aborted` (`mass_record_failure`) with the partial reason secondary; real failure after page k → `failed`, pages 1..k kept, resume at k+1. Progress is kept only through the last fully completed page (the listing request and every child request of its tickets), with the page's raw events and the cursor committed in one transaction; the updated-since window anchor persists across the runs of one pass; `lastSuccessfulSyncAt` is set only by the run that completes the final page and its normalization. A partial run never releases a held breach alert and never clears the stale marker (D13(b)). A persisted run is **superseded** by a later clean check (`lastSuccessfulSyncAt > run.finishedAt`, one clock reading written in the same update), after which it no longer causes Needs attention or Catching up and its failed-ticket list is not shown; integration-level causes (`reauth_required`, `permission_denied`) are read from `Integration.status`. Failed tickets are retried on every later pass (the incremental pass includes the persisted failed-id set). A zero-progress run is `partial` the first times; after three in a row it is `failed` (`no_progress`).
- **§6.13 Customer-facing sync states (R6, D32):** exactly one is shown, derived server-side (`lib/custom-provider/status.ts`): **Importing history** (`backfillCompletedAt` null, recent runs `ok` or `partial` with progress, no failure streak; never says failed or current), **Catching up** (initial import complete, latest runs `partial` with progress, source has an incremental cursor), **Up to date** (latest pass `ok` and fresh; failed tickets listed if any), **Needs attention** (real failure, guard abort, ceiling, `no_progress`, or `partial` for a source with no incremental cursor; error styling, never reported as a slow import), **Paused / disabled** (operator pause or availability). The stale-data marker (D13/D22) overlays any state. The integration page's Syncs section shows *Last checked* and *Last data update*; with no recorded change or failure it says no data changes were detected. Copy never states a supported capacity.

### §7 Raw payloads, personal data and retention

Stored: a whitelist projection of mapped fields and needed identifiers (the original key nesting is kept so the same path expressions evaluate against stored payloads), 64 KB per payload; unmapped fields (emails, phone numbers, attachments, internal notes) are never stored; sample responses are never stored server-side; the latest snapshot a live case needs is kept; **no raw event is deleted by this feature** and pruning of superseded snapshots waits on H-5 (`NormalizedEvent.sourceRawEventId` is `ON DELETE RESTRICT`, so any future prune may delete only unreferenced rows). Drafts and draft secrets expire after 14 days; sync runs after 30. **Disconnect** is the existing soft disconnect (credentials cleared, `status = disconnected`, `activeConfigVersion` and every version kept, all data kept and hidden by `packages/db/src/integration-visibility.ts`). **Reconnect (2026-10-11):** `POST /api/integrations/custom/reconnect` with fresh credentials only, on `Integration.activeConfigVersion` (never the highest version, never a draft), by compare-and-set in one transaction with a `reconnect` audit row; no draft is read and no version is created; activating a draft while a kept configuration is disconnected is refused (`reconnect_first`). **Delete configuration (2026-10-11, configuration only, no data purge):** a separate owner action offered only while disconnected (`disconnect_first` otherwise); deletes the draft and every configuration version and clears `activeConfigVersion`, `slaSupport` and credentials; everything else is kept (the `Integration` row, cases, commitments, evaluations, events, raw and normalized events, sync history, cursor, activation audit); setting up again starts at version 1 on the same row and the first activation shows the dry-run diff against kept cases. Conversation rendering uses an optional config-loading hook on `ProviderWebAdapter` (configured body and author paths at render time, so replay is preserved); message bodies are stored only if a body path is mapped.

### §8 Security

- **§8.1 Destination rules** (every request, web and worker): WHATWG parse, `https:` only, port 443 only, no userinfo, length-limited; reject IP-literal hosts, `localhost`, single-label hosts and `.local`/`.internal`/`.localhost` names; resolve and require **every** address to be public (`isPublicAddress`, `packages/email/src/destination.ts`, plus explicit denies for metadata names and `fd00:ec2::254`); a deployment-level local-development override, never per organization; the ticket-URL template host (a separate hostname) must be HTTPS and public, matched by exact hostname equality, used only to recognize and display links, never as a request destination.
- **§8.2 Pinning:** `undici@7.30.0` (exact-pinned in `@sla/safe-http`; 8.x needs a newer Node than the images) with `Agent({ connect: { lookup } })` so resolution and validation happen inside the connect path (the address connected to is the address validated; dual-stack returns the whole validated set and refuses if any record is denied); TLS hostname verification and SNI stay on; **one agent per ingest run**, closed at the end, short keep-alive, never shared across runs or tenants; use `undici.request`, not `fetch` (no decompression, redirects not followed, streamed byte cap, `Accept-Encoding: identity`); proxy environment variables are ignored. Limitations: a customer API behind a corporate proxy is unreachable; no HTTP/2. The N9.1 spike passed all eight criteria (`pnpm --filter @sla/safe-http spike`).
- **§8.3 Request rules:** only `GET`, or `POST` with a JSON body on an endpoint the owner confirmed as a read-only search (enforced by schema and at request time and before test/sample/preview); redirects disabled (a 3xx is an error); a next URL or `Link` target must be same-origin and pass §8.1; custom header names exclude `Host`, `Content-Length`, `Transfer-Encoding`, `Connection`, `Cookie`, `X-Forwarded-*`, and CR/LF is rejected; credential-looking query keys (`key`, `token`, `secret`, `password`, `auth`, `signature`, `credential`) are rejected and a query value equal to a stored secret is rejected at request time; template variables substitute only as in §4.2.
- **§8.4 Secrets and redaction (Q8, Q16):** secrets are encrypted separately (`Integration.credentials.secrets`, encrypted draft until activation), never returned to the UI (only "set / not set"), never in configuration versions. `packages/db/src/custom-secrets.ts`: strict per-value AES-256-GCM with the `enc:v1:` wire format and `INTEGRATION_TOKEN_ENCRYPTION_KEY` (a distinct scrypt salt), bound by GCM additional authenticated data to `custom-secret:v1\0<organizationId>\0<integrationId>\0<field>` (a draft uses the literal integration id `draft`, re-encrypted at activation); it refuses to write or read an unprefixed value; every malformed form (wrong part count, bad base64url, wrong IV/tag length, tampered ciphertext or tag, wrong binding) fails with the single generic `CustomCredentialsUnreadableError` (no `cause`), never returns plaintext, and has no plaintext fallback. Existing providers' encryption is untouched. **Rotation limitation:** changing the key makes every custom credential unreadable at once; owners re-enter them until N8-S7 exists (documented in `docs/deployment.md`). Decryption failure does not call the source and surfaces as a reconnect request with a generic message. Redaction: credentials, authorization headers, sensitive URLs and payload data are removed from logs, Sentry (`@sla/logger` scrubber, worker Sentry, web `scrubSentryEvent`), validation errors (built from field paths and fixed codes, never the submitted value), sync history, `lastSyncError` and API/UI responses; custom client errors carry only `{status, endpointKey, classification}`. Verification that must exist before Beta (recorded PASS in the ledger, B-10 and D-08): every stored secret starts `enc:v1:`; a seeded sentinel never appears anywhere; strict-helper rejections; cross-organization/integration/field rejection; existing-provider compatibility; the rotation note.
- **§8.5 Outbound-request endpoints** (test, sample, preview, validate, dry-run): owner-only (`requireOwner`), same-origin check, rate-limited per organization (`checkRateLimit`), one request in flight per organization, and only safe classifications (`ok`, `blocked_destination`, `unreachable`, `timeout`, `tls_error`, `auth_failed`, `forbidden`, `rate_limited`, `bad_response`); a blocked destination never reveals which check failed or what the name resolved to.
- **§8.6 Tenant isolation:** every new model carries `organizationId`, is read through organization-scoped helpers, and has an entry in `apps/web/test/tenant-scope-classification.test.ts` and seeding in `tenant-isolation.test.ts` (the first test fails for any model without a classification).
- **§8.7 Operator Beta flag (Q5; amended by D33):** the flag is the organization's entry on the Custom REST Beta allowlist, default off. Turning it off: no new outbound request or retry (checked before a run, before every attempt including each retry and back-off wake-up, and at every page boundary); an in-flight request is aborted within about 5 s (the run re-checks every 5 s) and its response discarded (run ends `aborted`, `flag_disabled`, now through the availability resolver); the same mutation sets `pollingPausedAt` so the worker skips ingest as a pause rather than counting a failing sync; every manual outbound route refuses (`integration_beta_restricted`); data stays visible and DB-only stages (normalization, commitments, evaluation, alerts) continue, the integration going stale (D22); turning it back on does not resume a pause set separately (use Resume polling).

### §9 Architecture and database

- **§9.1 Packages:** `@sla/safe-http` (shared address classification extracted from `@sla/email`, which re-exports it unchanged; the pinned client; limits and budgets). `@sla/custom-ticket` (Zod schema, path subset, transforms, validators, ingest engine, the pure `deriveBatch(config, rows)` used by preview, dry-run and the worker, the adapter and web adapter); it imports only types and errors from `@sla/ingestion`, never the projector, and writes no domain table. Registered once as `custom`. Capabilities: `webhooks`, `policyImport`, `calendarImport`, `priorityChanges`, `officialLinks` false; `replyEvents` true only in Full SLA; `incrementalNormalization` false until §6.10's evidence exists.
- **§9.2 Registration checklist for a new provider (also the N7 checklist).** Compile-forced: `IntegrationProvider` enum (migration with the `ADD VALUE` pattern), `apps/worker/src/providers.ts`, `apps/web/src/lib/providers.ts` (`PROVIDERS`, `WEB_PROVIDERS`), `INTEGRATION_PROVIDER_LABELS` in `apps/web/src/lib/types/integrations.ts`, `PROVIDER_ICONS` in `IntegrationDetailHeader.tsx`, `PRESENTATION` in `provider-presentation.tsx`, and the catalog entry in `packages/db/src/integration-catalog.ts`. **Needed but not compile-forced (a miss is a silent bug):** the hand-written `IntegrationProvider` union, `INTEGRATION_PROVIDERS` and `IntegrationsPageData`; `getIntegrationsData`; `source-integration-specs.tsx` (real data; the existing cards show static placeholders, do not copy them); `lib/onboarding-data.ts` (iterates every registered provider; custom is excluded from onboarding in V1); `lib/security-summary.ts` (iterates `WEB_PROVIDERS`; custom needs `access.scopes = []` and a note that read-only is enforced by request rules); `packages/db/src/plans.ts` integration lines (Starter, Team); entitlements (no code change expected, `integrationResource` counts by role; verify); the boundary test's `PROVIDER_PACKAGES`; admin and dashboard labels. **Deliberately not extended for custom:** `ConfigurableIntegrationProvider`/`IntegrationConfig` (no OAuth client), `CONNECT_LINK_PROVIDERS` (trackers only), the five per-provider OAuth route sets (custom has one route family `/api/integrations/custom/*`), `REPORT_TICKET_URL_COLUMN`, `TICKET_SOURCE_KEY_PREFIXES`.
- **§9.3 Database (additive, all applied):** `IntegrationProvider` += `custom`; `CustomProviderDraft` (unique per organization in V1, 14-day `expiresAt`); `CustomProviderConfigVersion` (immutable, no secrets, `configHash`, unique `(integrationId, version)`); `Integration.activeConfigVersion`, `slaSupport`, `lastDataChangedAt`; `IntegrationSyncRun` (index `(integrationId, startedAt)`); `GuardOverride`; `CustomActivationAudit`; `Organization.customProviderEnabled` (superseded by the allowlist, dropped by N10-F1). New models need tenant-scope classification.
- **§9.4 Touch points:** the organization's ticket-URL template is copied at activation into `Integration.credentials` as plaintext (non-secret, like Zendesk's `subdomain`), so `recognizeCaseUrl(url, credentials)` needs no contract change (exact hostname plus an id captured from the path); guards live inside the pure `normalize()`; deletion is a `ticket_deleted` raw event written by ingest; sync-run and record-failure recording for all providers is an additive change to `apps/worker/src/cycle.ts` (it previously dropped `normalization.failures` for every provider); the optional `partial` marker on `IngestResult` is the only shared contract change.
- **§9.5 Multi-instance later:** nothing new keys on `(organizationId, provider)` (code addresses the integration by `integrationId`); a later expand migration adds `Integration.instanceKey TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT ''` and replaces the unique key with `(organizationId, provider, instanceKey)` (26 non-test files mention `organizationId_provider`); known couplings to lift: `Case.system`/policy `sourceKey` is the constant `custom`, `CustomerIdentity.kind` needs an instance prefix, ticket-URL recognizers are keyed by provider name, labels come from `INTEGRATION_PROVIDER_LABELS`; the drafts table's per-organization unique is the only place V1 hard-wires "one".

### §10 Shared changes under the D24 gate

Everything else in N9 is additive and isolated in the new packages. Shared: sync-run recording and failure surfacing for all providers (N9.9) and the no-change skip with `lastDataChangedAt` (D32; the projector only gains read-only change counts); the optional `partial` ingest outcome and its `cycle.ts` branch (success still advances `lastSuccessfulSyncAt` and resets the streak, failure still increments it, `partial` does neither; D13(b) unchanged); commitment-kind exclusion and cancellation (N9.8a; first code to cancel `first_response`/`resolution`); redaction (output only); the `ProviderWebAdapter` config hook (optional member); the alert caveat (`@sla/notifications`, D24 replay); the `@sla/email` re-export. `@sla/core` is untouched. Each carries focused regression coverage and D24 replay where applicable.

### §13–§14 Verification and rollout

Verification follows `CLAUDE.md` (focused tests by file; no full suite per PR; N9 changes no CI trigger; Q9 deferred). Focused coverage recorded PASS before the Beta pilot (ledger B-03 to B-16, D-08: 31 files, 598 tests): SSRF and the client, mapping, current-state-versus-history, SLA exclusion for all three entry paths, SLA modes, every guard boundary row, ceiling, budget, partial runs and failure classification, activation blocking, customer-facing states, commitment cancellation and rollback, override paths, the Beta flag, secrets, synchronization, deletion signals, tenant isolation, no-change runs, and regression for existing providers. **Rollout:** Beta (D17), operator-enabled per organization (allowlist), default off; pilot with one or two design partners; customer, marketing and setup documentation is updated only after each claim is verified against the implementation, and Terms and Privacy edits are never made without the legal reviewer (see the gate below).

### §15 Open items and risks

Open owner items: the legal-review gate (below); option (a) of U2 before general availability; the first pilot allowlist entry (which organization; it does not wait for the legal gate, which gates only the Terms, Privacy and public copy); the real-host benchmark before raising the ceiling; the `D34` label (below). Proposed details not owner decisions: the exact `GuardOverride` field list and the 24 h expiry (settled in N9.11 but not separately approved), customer-facing copy until verified. **Risks kept:** capacity unproven beyond the Beta ceiling; a wrong creation actor freezes a wrong first-response clock; `deletedAt` is never cleared (a restored ticket stays hidden); unpruned snapshots grow without bound until H-5; Resolution accuracy for current-status-only sources is limited and labelled; a guard abort repeats until resolved (a mass-deletion abort has no override); a key rotation makes every custom credential unreadable; per-ticket comment requests make large imports take hours; no CI on PRs into `main`; legal copy cannot be finalized without review.

- **Status: OPEN.** Qualified legal review (validation ledger D-07 / BL-09) is a separate required gate and is **not satisfied** by any owner decision below. The owner's **legal-review hold stays in force** until the owner explicitly closes it: no Terms, Privacy, legal-review or decision-sheet content is to be edited, published or reinterpreted by engineering, and no legal approval is claimed anywhere. The live `TermsView.tsx` and `PrivacyView.tsx` were never edited by N9. `docs/custom-beta-deployment.md` records the hold.
- **Owner decisions recorded 2026-10-10** (product decisions, not legal approval; the decision of record is ledger section 2.4): **O-1 Option A**, align the retention and privacy wording to the implemented behavior, no purge (data kept for the organization's lifetime; disconnect stops reading and keeps data; no erasure-on-request promise); **O-2 Option A** (changed from Option B later the same day), no fixed-egress-IP requirement for the initial launch, fixed-egress work deferred indefinitely, no customer-facing promise about the address, and the Elastic IP `13.62.74.24` (which also serves the application's public URL) must not be released or replaced; **O-3 Option A**, POST search endpoints stay and wording must not imply POST is read-only. The reviewer receives these as fixed inputs.

Engineering drafts **W-1** (retention), **W-2** (read-only/POST) and **W-3** (outbound IP, unused under O-2 Option A) exist only in git history. **The full reviewer package (verified facts F-1 to F-17 with code references, the proposed wording, the decision record to be filled in by the reviewer) was in `implementation-plans/n9-legal-review.md` and `n9-legal-decision-sheet.md`, last present at `d0fdc78`; recover it from there when the owner closes the hold.**

## N10 design record

> Migrated from plan 10. The operator runbook is [`docs/integration-availability.md`](../docs/integration-availability.md); D33 holds the owner's rulings. Code: `packages/db/src/integration-availability.ts` and `integration-catalog.ts`, `apps/web/src/lib/integration-availability.ts` and `admin-integration-availability.ts`.

- **§4.1 States.** `enabled` is independent of `releaseStage` (`stable`, `beta`, `coming_soon`; "Disabled" is not a stage); a Beta provider has `betaAccess` `all_organizations` or `allowlist`. `enabled = false` → nobody; `stable` → every organization; `beta` + `all_organizations` → every organization, Beta label; `beta` + `allowlist` → only listed organizations; `coming_soon` → nobody can connect or operate it. One resolver answers "is this provider available to this organization now?": `available`, or unavailable with `integration_disabled`, `integration_coming_soon` or `integration_beta_restricted`.
- **§4.2 Catalog versus policy.** `INTEGRATION_CATALOG satisfies Record<IntegrationProvider, …>` (display name, category, connection type, default availability) is static (D16); the policy is data (`IntegrationAvailability`, one row per provider, `version` for compare-and-set; `IntegrationBetaAllowlist`, primary key `(provider, organizationId)`); a missing row falls back to the catalog default. Coming Soon applies only to providers in the enum. Seed: Zendesk, Jira, Linear stable; Intercom and GitHub beta + `all_organizations`; `custom` beta + `allowlist` seeded from `customProviderEnabled = true` organizations. `Organization.customProviderEnabled` was kept unread and is dropped by N10-F1.
- **§5.1 Resolver.** `getIntegrationAvailabilityPolicy`, `listIntegrationAvailability`, `resolveIntegrationAvailability(db, organizationId, provider)` (two indexed reads at most), `resolveOrganizationAvailability`; **no cache**: every check reads the database, so a change applies to the next check in every process; no new infrastructure.
- **§5.2 Web.** `requireIntegrationAvailable` returns 403 `{ error, code, provider }`; OAuth GET routes use `availabilityRedirect` to `/settings/integrations?availability=<code>&provider=<provider>`. Gated after authentication and before any provider call or write: `{provider}/connect` (redirect or 403), `{provider}/callback` (redirect; the code is not exchanged, no credentials stored, a connect link not consumed), `connect-links` create (403) and `connect-links/[token]/start` (redirect, link not consumed), `{provider}/config` POST and DELETE (GET stays readable), `{provider}/backfill`, `webhooks/{zendesk,jira}/[integrationId]` (**200** `{ status: "ignored", reason }` after the secret check, before parsing, nothing stored), `concierge/{zendesk,jira}/export`, and `custom/*` via `ownerGuard` (`status` and `disconnect` pass `requireAvailable: false`: no outbound request, and a customer can always see and disconnect a paused source). A static boundary test fails if a route under `app/api/integrations/**`, `app/api/webhooks/**` or `app/api/concierge/**/export` neither calls the helper nor is on the exemption list (disconnect routes, Slack, connect-link revoke).
- **§5.3 Worker.** `processOrganization` reads `resolveOrganizationAvailability` once per organization run before ingest; an unavailable integration is skipped exactly like the N4.5 operator pause (no provider call, no sync attempt recorded, no failure, no `failingSince`; log `integration_ingest_unavailable` with the code). Normalization, commitments, evaluation and alerts on stored data keep running and the source goes stale (D22; D13(b) holds breach alerts). `runCustomIngest` uses the resolver before the run and in its 5 s in-flight check. Built-in providers have no in-flight abort (a running call finishes its current bounded run); there is no job queue, so "queued" work is the next scheduled run, which re-checks. An unreadable policy fails closed (no provider call, stored data still evaluated).
- **§5.4 Custom REST allowlist.** Removing an organization from the Custom REST allowlist sets `pollingPausedAt` on its custom integration in the same transaction; re-adding never resumes a pause. An allowlist add is scoped to that organization, validated and audited. The catalog carries no rollout block (N9.14-F1 and its block were removed on 2026-10-12, Rev 17): the admin API applies the same rules to Custom REST as to every provider.
- **§5.5 Customer-facing.** The settings page and onboarding selector show an unavailable provider as Unavailable or Coming soon (with the operator's message), with no connect control; a Beta-restricted provider is hidden from organizations not on its allowlist; a connected integration whose provider becomes unavailable shows **"Paused by Elapsed"**, never "Disconnected" (`Integration.status` is never changed; the customer may still disconnect but not reconnect); the Beta label derives from `releaseStage`.
- **§5.6 Documentation and the stage.** The persisted `releaseStage` is the only source of an integration's Beta/Stable/Coming soon wording. `apps/web/src/lib/release-stage.ts` holds the wording, `components/shared/release-stage.tsx` the badge and notice, and `lib/release-stage-data.ts` reads the policy for pages with no organization (the public `/docs/integrations/*` pages for the five registered providers, their search descriptions and the home page integration list, all rendered per request). Settings, onboarding and the Custom REST page use the same badge. Beta shows a label and a short early-release notice; Stable shows a neutral label on documentation pages and no warning; no copy is edited on a promotion, and a stage is not a connection status or sync health. The Markdown guides are not rendered by the app and carry no stage. See `docs/integration-availability.md`, "Where the stage is shown". Slack has no stage (not a registered provider).
- **§6 Lifecycle.** Disabling leaves integration rows, credentials, cursors, webhook secrets, raw events, cases, events, commitments, evaluations and notifications unchanged; re-enabling repairs nothing: the next run ingests from the stored cursor (Zendesk's incremental export `start_time`, Jira's `updated >= updatedSince` JQL recover ignored webhooks), and an integration that was `disconnected`, `reauth_required` or operator-paused stays that way. Availability (`IntegrationAvailability` / `IntegrationBetaAllowlist`) and disconnect (`Integration.status`) are different columns and never written by the same action.
- **§7 Admin console and API (platform operators only, `requirePlatformOperator`).** `/admin/integrations` (table per provider: stage, Beta access and allowlist size, connections, active organizations, health, last update and operator, edit dialog with a required reason and an impact preview before narrowing; allowlist panel; 409 `stale_version` reloads the row). `GET /api/admin/integrations`; `PATCH …/providers/[provider]` (`{ expectedVersion, enabled?, releaseStage?, betaAccess?, statusMessage?, reason }`; 400 invalid, 409 `stale_version`, a no-op writes nothing); `POST …/providers/[provider]/impact`; `POST` and `DELETE …/providers/[provider]/allowlist[/organizationId]`; the legacy `POST /api/admin/tenants/[organizationId]/custom-provider` is a thin wrapper kept for one release; the per-integration controls route is unchanged. **§7.2 Audit:** one `AdminAuditLog` row per write, in the same transaction: `update_integration_availability` (`{ provider, before, after, reason }`), `add_integration_allowlist`, `remove_integration_allowlist` (`{ provider, organizationId, reason, pausedPolling }`); never a credential or configuration payload; the legacy `enable_custom_provider` / `disable_custom_provider` actions stay readable. **§7.3 Concurrency:** `PATCH` is `updateMany({ where: { provider, version: expectedVersion } })` with `version` incremented (zero rows → 409 `stale_version`); a missing row inserts the seeded default first in the same transaction; an allowlist duplicate is 409 `already_listed`.
- **§8 Security.** Only `PLATFORM_ADMIN_EMAILS` operators can read or change availability; owners and members get 403 from the API and `notFound()` from the page; no organization-level setting overrides a platform restriction; admin data is aggregated counts and policy fields, credentials are never selected; the customer-facing message is operator text rendered as text.
- **§10 Tests (focused, on `testing`, D33 ruling 8).** Suites as built: `packages/db/test/integration-availability.test.ts` (unit) and `integration-availability.db.test.ts` (two pools, real database), `packages/custom-ticket/test/ingest-availability.db.test.ts`, `apps/web/test/integration-availability-admin.test.ts`, `integration-availability-routes.test.ts`, `integration-availability-boundary.test.ts` (static route boundary), `admin-integrations-view.test.ts` (static render; the repo has no DOM test environment, so the refresh-error and toast paths were checked in a browser), `apps/worker/test/integration-availability.test.ts`; the four real-database suites are listed in `vitest.config.ts`. Coverage: every §4.1 state and missing-row defaults; admin authorization (owner, member and signed-out refused on every route), persistence with one audit row and none for a no-op; two writes with the same `expectedVersion` (one 409); each blocked Custom REST change refused with 409 and nothing written, while narrowing changes and an allowlist add under Beta/allowlist succeed; connect, callback and connect-link start refusing without storing credentials or consuming the link; webhook 200 ignored; backfill, config writes, concierge export and custom routes 403 with the code; worker skipping like a pause with stored-data stages still running and re-enable ingesting on the next run; allowlist removal blocking on the next check; disabling and re-enabling leaving rows, credentials, cases, events and commitments byte-identical; propagation without a cache; the route boundary test.
- **§11 Rollback.** Additive schema. After N10-F1 the column is gone, so pre-N10 code cannot run without restoring it (`ALTER TABLE "organizations" ADD COLUMN "customProviderEnabled" BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT false`, then re-set it from the Custom REST allowlist); allowlist additions made after the deploy are not reflected, so re-check the Custom REST flag after a rollback.
- **§12 Out of scope.** Percentage rollout, an "internal organizations" concept (use the allowlist), Slack, per-plan availability, database-backed admin roles, an in-flight abort for built-in providers, listing providers that have no adapter.
